## v76 — "boss-ready first run" (Oct 14, shipped)
Builds on the boss first-run QA below (findings F1–F9), the owner's Connect two-card directive (in the v75 section), and a same-evening field report: Suggested dispatches — tapping Use "does nothing". New suite tools/boss-firstrun-test.js 139/139; full run 25 suites + OCR harness (32/32) green, except dispatch-autostart's pre-existing wall-clock fixture (same failure NAME as the contemporaneous baseline, 57 passed / 1 failed at ship time — no NEW failures). Two role-package assertions that encoded the superseded office order were updated with the Oct 14 owner blueprint cited (suite still 109/109). Deliberately NOT touched: P&L/Maintenance access gates (owner's Oct 13 direction), the autostart engine, manifest collect() (v75), F10.
- FIXED F1 (HIGH): the unanswered-role trap is closed three ways. (1) Onboarding movers gate on the question: Next will not advance past "Do you drive?" while unanswered (a plain nudge renders AT the question), Skip lands ON the question instead of past it, and finishOnboarding holds there too — the v52 default-stamp inside finishOnboarding is GONE. (2) ensureUserRole never stamps 'both': 'both' is a driver face and is never assigned on a guess (documented choice). (3) App.userRole() resolves an unanswered Owner-Operator to the PROVISIONAL office face (new provisionalRole helper) until he answers — office order, no tracker. Answered picks remain never overridden; the Driver/Fleet-Manager default migrations and roleDefaultFor/resolveUserRole semantics are unchanged.
- FIXED F2: onboarding is role-branched — an 'office' answer skips the park-place, Truck details, Odometer photo and Safety sticker steps entirely (ONB_OFFICE_SKIP; render, Next, Back and system-back all walk visible steps only; progress counts visible steps). An office user never sees driver homework.
- FIXED F3: Dispatch has an office face — the save button is a plain "Save" (no "Save & start" rendered at all), the active card reads "Saved on this device" (no "● In progress", no Started row), History offers no Start button, and ONE honest line sits in the form view: "Dispatches are saved on this device — sending them to your drivers arrives with Connect Phase 2." Driver/both rendering is unchanged (suite-proven both ways).
- FIXED F4: the office home + sidebar lead with the boss's five tools in blueprint order — Connect, Dispatch, Invoice, Maintenance, P&L (same ten modules, emphasis only).
- FIXED F5: Revenue's Tracker auto-start card + its Save button render only for non-office roles (the engine hard-returns 'office' and could never fire — the card was an inert promise); the save wiring is null-guarded.
- FIXED F6: Preferences Truck card hides the odometer-baseline photo flow and the "Daily odometer photos" tracker toggle for office users (truck number/type and the safety sticker stay — fleet facts an office manages). The save handler guards the absent fields and keeps stored values.
- FIXED F7 + CONNECT RESTRUCTURE (owner directive, Oct 14 19:15): modules/connect.js rewritten to exactly TWO cards — (1) Current connections (list + Add; each connection's 📋 Send dispatch / 📎 Send doc opens an inline picker ON that connection), (2) History (the outbox rendered as history; queued items pill "Queued — on this device"). The "Phase 1 — local only" banner card and the standalone "Share something" card are deleted; the local-only honesty is ONE quiet line inside the connections card. The picker names dispatches by jobSite (fallback site/destination/desc — the old chain missed jobSite, so every real dispatch listed as a bare "date — Dispatch"); a share now packages the ACTUAL content into the outbox item (dispatch: jobSite, scheduledAt, equipment, material, ticketNo, notes + refId; the label carries the real name) — no more content-free stubs. Native alert()/confirm() are gone: inline add-form error, inline two-step Remove confirm, one-shot flash line. Store keys con_list/con_outbox unchanged; legacy outbox stubs still render in History.
- FIXED F8: onboarding's Company step (all roles) collects Country + Province/State — Canadian provinces as a code select — into onboarding.country / onboarding.province, the fields Invoice has always read; finishOnboarding mirrors a province code to homeProvince when unset. Invoice also bridges at read time (never mutating the record): a stored homeProvince full name from Preferences satisfies the jurisdiction check (country Canada + province code), so pre-v76 records stop meeting the red "Set your location first" banner too. GST table and prefill behavior otherwise untouched.
- FIXED F9: the "🧪 Beta testing" banner no longer renders for office users. Display is decoupled from the gate — BETA_MODE still sets the premium tier and tierAllows reads the flag itself, so the tools stay unlocked; only the developer wording leaves his first screen.
- FIXED — SUGGESTED DISPATCHES "Use does nothing" (owner field report, Oct 14 19:22 MDT): DIAGNOSIS — the defect IS in the shipped web build (v75 and earlier); it is NOT stale-bundle-only. DispatchIngest.use() itself was sound — the draft WAS written through the v48 machinery (already proven by dispatch-ingest-test §e) and the form WAS refilled on render. The failure was VISIBILITY, twice: (1) on success, the handler re-rendered and fired a ONE-SHOT scrollIntoView on the Job site field — the exact pattern v75 proved dead on the phone for Edit and replaced with dspScrollToForm (the ingest card re-paints at render end and ticket photos decode, shifting layout after the single scroll). The form's only confirmation ("📝 Draft restored") waited off-screen while the suggestion vanished from the list: at the driver's seat, "nothing happened." (2) On a busy-draft REFUSAL, the explanation rendered at the BOTTOM of the suggestions card beside the paste box (ingestFlash) — off-screen from the tapped Use button, indistinguishable from a dead button. FIX (modules/dispatch.js only): a successful Use names the load in the form's own banner ("📩 Loaded from your suggestions: <job site · time> — check the form, then tap Save. Nothing is saved until you do."), posts a pointer line at the top of the suggestions card, and brings the form to the user with dspScrollToForm; refusals now render AT THE TAP in a new top-of-card slot. Verified Save remains the only record-creating step — proven end-to-end in boss-firstrun-test (d)/(d2): real intake → real Use click in a fake DOM → draft + populated form fields + banner + scroll, Save creates exactly ONE record filed under the suggestion's date, today's active dispatch byte-untouched; a busy form refuses with the suggestion and draft intact and the reason at the tap.
- STILL OPEN: F10 (park-place nudges have no office gate — latent; cannot fire on a fresh store). The two manifest threads from the field report above (17:38 record origin; 17:39 odometer screen) were explicitly out of scope for v76 and remain open.

## v75 — "records you can open" (Oct 14, shipped)
- FIXED (Revenue): the period chart's bars are tappable (each bar opens its day), and the main 📒 Entries / ✨ Extras rows open their day too (a tap on a row's own Delete button still only deletes). Day Details now EDIT as well as view/delete: every entry and extra gets ✏️ Edit → a prefilled form at the top of the detail (type, amount, hours/loads/miles/tonnes/trips, note) → Save changes writes VERIFIED (read-back byte-compare, v48 rule) — a failed write shows a plain in-page error, keeps the typed edit in the reopened form (dayEditDraft), and changes nothing. Day-detail deletes are verified the same way. While an edit is open, other rows render no Delete buttons (a delete would shift the indexes the edit points at). Entries/extras keep every field the form doesn't own (date, auto, photo…).
- FIXED (Fuel): Fill-ups rows are tappable → the form opens in EDIT mode prefilled (date, odometer, litres, cost, station; receipt photo shown when the record carries one; fields marked touched so OCR can't rewrite them), "💾 Save changes" updates the SAME entry in place (no duplicate), verified the same read-back way; Cancel edit is a quiet ghost. The add path is verified now too.
- FIXED (Fuel guard): a fill with BOTH litres and cost empty (or zero/non-numeric) can no longer save — new or edited. Blocked with a plain in-form message ("Enter the litres or the cost — a fill-up with neither can't be saved.") and the form is NOT re-rendered, so nothing typed is lost. The existing blank Oct 13 entry on the phone can now be fixed by tapping it and filling it in.
- FIXED (Dispatch): Edit now brings the form to the user — dspScrollToForm scrolls to the form card immediately, on the next frame, and once more at +350 ms (the old one-shot scrollIntoView did not survive the phone's late layout: ingest paint + ticket-photo decode). The tapped History item is also marked at the tap point (orange frame, "✏️ You're editing this one — the form is at the top of this page ↑", button reads "✏️ Editing ↑").
- FIXED (Manifest "Invalid Date"): ROOT CAUSE PROVEN from code — app.js stores session endedAt as an ISO STRING (toISOString), but collect() computed the start as `endedAt - seconds*1000` on the raw value → NaN → new Date(NaN) renders literally "Invalid Date", for EVERY saved session. (The older test fixtures used numeric endedAt, which is why the suites never saw it.) collect() now parses every stamp to ms first (stampMs/fmtStamp/fmtStartOf); unparseable values render "—", never "Invalid Date". Display only — start is still endedAt − seconds, end is still endedAt.
- STILL OPEN (Manifest end time): the "End 5:38 p.m. though I never stopped" half is NOT a display bug and v75 does not change it: the end shown is the last SAVED session's endedAt, so a session record ending 17:38 existed on the phone. Who wrote it (stale phone bundle vs an orphaned session) is still unproven — the owner's cold-restart check (does the tracker show RUNNING or STOPPED after reopening?) remains the deciding test.
- STILL OPEN (Fuel odo message): the 17:39 "Enter a valid odometer reading" screenshot is unaccounted for — the current Fuel form has no such validation (odometer is optional since v44 and no string in the module matches). Not touched in v75; needs the owner to identify which screen produced it.
- Tests: NEW tools/records-open-test.js 87/87 (manifest ISO fixtures, fuel guard/edit/verified-save round-trips incl. the blank Oct 13 entry filled in place, revenue edit/verified saves driving dayDetailData, dispatch scroll attempts + tap-point marker). Full run: 24 suites + OCR harness — 1,599 checks passing; dispatch-autostart-test keeps its 5 PRE-EXISTING wall-clock failures (53 passed; identical before this ship — its "missed start" fixtures assume a morning clock).
- CONNECT LAYOUT — OWNER DIRECTIVE (Oct 14, 19:15 MDT, over his phone screenshot): "No need for 4 cards here... Only 2 — one current connections and one history." Current screen stacks FOUR cards (Phase-1 banner / My connections / Share something / Outbox). New law for the module: (1) CURRENT CONNECTIONS card — connections list + Add; sharing lives INSIDE a connection (tap a connection -> share to them), the standalone "Share something" card dies. (2) HISTORY card — everything sent/received with its tick state; todays Outbox becomes History (queued items show as pending in history). The "Phase 1 — local only" banner is developer plumbing on the users face — REMOVE it from the module UI entirely (plumbing rule).

## OTA lag diagnosis — Oct 14, 2026 (evening): phone renders pre-v75 after v75 is live
Full report: ~/workspace/goals/dump-truck-haul-ticket-app/hidden_files/ota-lag-diagnosis-oct14.md. Diagnosis only — no code changed.
- ROOT CAUSE (high confidence): OTA is checked exactly ONCE per cold page load (js/ota.js bootCheck, gated by app.js on OTA.ready()). No polling, no visibilitychange/resume re-check. v75 shipped ~18:44 MDT while the app was in continuous use; the sticky ShiftTrackingService foreground service keeps the process — and the warm WebView page — alive all shift, so the running pre-v75 copy never re-checked. A "not yet", not a "cannot": the same path delivered v65–v74 to this phone.
- VERIFIED FROM THE SHIPPED v34 APK (assets/public/): bundles webVersion 64; modules/manifest.js sha 43cae4b2… carries the proven line-42 `endedAt - seconds*1000` bug; js/ota.js sha f4465cb3… is byte-identical to the current hardened (v35-integrity) updater. capacitor.config.json has NO server.url (bundled-first + OTA redirect); MainActivity is stock BridgeActivity — no WebView cache-mode/interception pinning; BootReceiver/ShiftTrackingService never hold a WebView (service is START_STICKY, WebView-independent by design). sw.js is a self-unregistering eviction SW — not a suspect.
- MINIMAL USER FIX (in order): (0) Preferences → ⚙️ Advanced — the note under "Check for updates" shows the running version or a parked ⚠️ ota_notice. (1) PREFERRED: that same button → OTA.checkNow() downloads v75 → confirm "Restart the app now" → done; zero data risk (code-only, localStorage/sign-in/sessions untouched). (2) Equivalent: swipe-close in Recents + reopen online — bootCheck auto-updates. (3) Settings → Apps → Clear CACHE is harmless but useless here (no HTTP-cache pin in the path). (4) ⛔ NEVER "Clear storage/data" for this: wipes localStorage + ota-web + sign-in; local-only/unsynced data would be lost (real risk given today's local-mode episode).
- NO NEW APK NEEDED: v34 shell picks up v75 as-is. The one structural gap is web-side and OTA-shippable: add a throttled on-resume update check (hook app.js visibilitychange → visible; background-download + restart confirm) so a continuously-running app can't lag a release for a whole work day.
- CLOSED DISPATCH EDIT — OWNER QUESTION (Oct 14, 19:24 MDT): "why is that edit still there — the Dispatch is closed?" A completed dispatch in History still offers a plain Edit + full edit form. PROPOSED LAW (put to owner, awaiting nod): closed dispatch opens as VIEW (read-only) first; changes go through a deliberate "Make a correction" step that states what will change and keeps the change history on the record (matches Connect blueprint: corrections marked on both books). Once a dispatch is INVOICED, corrections stop entirely — the period must be reopened instead. NOTE: tonight the owner legitimately needed to edit the closed Aecon dispatch to attach ticket 23177 — a hard lock with no correction door would have blocked that.
- Oct 15 (Asjad screenshot, 07:07): Suggested-dispatch card's relative-day label said "Tomorrow 8:00am" for TODAY's 8:00 AM dispatch on the morning of Oct 15 — relative label computed at ingest/render time and not refreshed at midnight. Cosmetic; underlying scheduledAt correct. Also: an unfinished form edit blocks Use with an orange banner explaining why (intended guard, v76-era behavior) — user read it as "Use still not working."
- Oct 15 (Muse browser task, verified in code + live): **A signed-out but already-onboarded user has NO reachable sign-in UI in the web app.** The #auth view only displays when the local `onboarded` flag is absent; Logout while signed out just clears the email key and reloads to the dashboard; Preferences Account/Security has no sign-in button; the sync dot only alerts status. Workaround (needs owner approval, NOT done): Preferences → Advanced → "Reset onboarding questions" → relaunch → auth landing appears → sign in with saved login; answers are preserved/pre-filled, but the cloud tombstone means the 10 setup questions reappear for a click-through. No data touched. Found while trying to sign Muse's browser copy into Asjad's cloud to upload the Oct 15 start-odo photo — photo still NOT uploaded; upload flow itself verified ready (dashboard START tracker opens the "📸 Starting odometer" sheet with Choose file + Odometer field + Save reading; note it starts the tracker).
- Oct 15, 08:02 MDT (user screenshot, phone v34): **Auto-start missed AGAIN + elapsed counter restarts on foreground.** Tracker shows "Working... 00:00:12 / Counting from 8:01 AM" at 08:02 with the dispatch due 8:00. Three defects: (1) the 8:00 dispatch/fallback auto-start did not fire while the app was asleep (repeat of Oct 14 — background robustness, Samsung battery killing); (2) on-open start landed at 8:01, not backdated to the 8:00 dispatch time; (3) the elapsed display reads ~12 s at 08:02 instead of counting continuously from the session start — the counter appears to restart when the app reaches foreground. User workaround given: Edit the start to 8:00 AM. Pay is unaffected (foreman ticket hours govern).
- Oct 15, ~08:05 MDT (user, by voice): **DESIGN DEFECT — live miles are gated on the session start.** User: "The miles tracker shouldn't be waiting for start time. I started driving already from the time I sent you the [odometer photo]" (~07:07 MDT, start odo 168,759). He drove home→site before the 8:00 dispatch start; the app showed Miles (live) 0.0 km at 08:02 — all pre-start driving invisible to the tracker. Against his settled three-windows doctrine (shift clock ≠ dispatch ≠ driving): live-miles/GPS accumulation must run from first motion of the workday (or the morning odo capture), never wait for the dispatch/session start time. The odometer day-delta tonight will carry the true distance; the live instrument must match that reality.
- Oct 15, 10:33 MDT (user screenshot + report): **Live miles still foreground-only.** Tracker (start correctly edited to 8:00 AM, elapsed 02:33:35 ✓) shows Miles (live) **15.9 km** after a morning of site driving. User: "this is tracking only km's when on screen again." Same root as Oct 14's ~13.7 km-after-100 km: background GPS dies when the app leaves the foreground (Samsung battery killing; no foreground-service). His standing hard requirement stands: battery saver must not be able to break background work. Odometer delta remains the true distance record meanwhile.
- Oct 15, 10:34 MDT (user notification-shade screenshot): BOTH Meticulous notifications present — "Meticulous is tracking your shift" (since 8:01 AM, "Live miles are being recorded, even with the app closed") and "Meticulous location" (10:33 AM, "recording your work trip location"). User pleased ("Nice! This is here again!"). Nuance for the miles defect: the foreground notifications DO persist/return, yet live miles still undercount (15.9 km by 10:33) — so the remaining gap is GPS sampling/throttling in background, not total service death.
- Oct 15, 10:37 MDT (user, dictated mid-drive): **REQUIREMENT — dump-site identity recognition.** "The app has to be smart enough to recognize if the user is on the same dump site as once he went to." His Oct 15 day proves the shape: THREE jobs in one day — dump on site A, then dump on a DIFFERENT site B, then left the site altogether. So the learned-places engine must: (1) fingerprint each dump site from the first tipped load (his Oct 14 law), (2) RECOGNIZE a return to a previously seen site vs a new one, (3) keep multiple distinct sites straight within a single day/dispatch, and (4) detect departure ("left the site altogether") to close the visit. All behind the glass — no user data entry, no site picking.

## v80 — "photo reads you can trust" (Oct 15, shipped)
- FIXED (OCR gate, two-sided): odometer candidates are judged against the master BOTH ways — below it, or more than 1,000 km past it (ODO_MAX_JUMP_KM), counts as a failed read: never filled, never persisted, escalates to Power Read, whose answers now pass the SAME gate centrally (escalate + queue drain) before any screen may use them. parseOdometerReading is plausible-first when a master exists (the dash beats a receipt's invoice number in the same frame). Hunt chips filter impossible candidates. Every surface words the rejection by direction ("below your current odometer" / "way past your current odometer").
- FIXED (master writes only on confirm): Preferences photo reads + the queued-power-read Use button now fill the field/draft only; mt_odo moves at Save preferences through App._odoSaveDecision — at/above sets, below applies ONLY with the new "This corrects a wrong odometer reading" box ticked (the supervised un-poisoning door), unarmed-and-lower keeps the master and says so. The Truck field renders the live master, not the stale setup-day baseline (the old render+save pair silently rewound the master on every Preferences save).
- FIXED (Power Read key loss): the key + switch mirror to a raw localStorage slot outside the mt_ namespace; a wiped store copy self-heals from the mirror, and the store copy still cloud-syncs for signed-in reinstalls. Preferences states the key's state at every paint ("✓ Key saved on this device." / "Power Read key not set — reads are on-device only."). The no-key escalation message carries the same plain sentence.
- FIXED (fuel receipt rescue): parseFuelReceipt gains a loose arithmetic-verified triple (labelled-column receipts — numbers separated by printed labels), label-reach litres/price across line breaks, garbled-TOTAL tolerance, a GST-line guard ("GST INCLUDED IN TOTAL $9.23" can no longer be taken as the total), and cost derivation (litres × price) on every path. The Fuel form now escalates to Power Read whenever litres or cost is missing, not only when nothing was found.
- Tests: NEW tools/odo-guard-test.js 67/67 (gate tables both files, plausible-first, save decision incl. un-poisoning a 519,326 master, mirror self-heal, gated cloud answers live + queued, receipt fixtures incl. the GST trap). power-read suites updated where they encoded the superseded v63 one-sided contract (cited in-suite); full run green except the two pre-existing wall-clock suites, identical to baseline.

## v82 — "Closing the dispatch closes the shift; intake never fights the form; loads exist" (Oct 15, shipped)
- FIXED (Dispatch close semantics): completing the ACTIVE dispatch — plain Complete or the close-with-ticket Confirm, both of which end in applyCompletion — now stops the running tracker session at that moment (App.stopSessionOnDispatchClose -> the same stopSessionAt as the tracker's own Stop, so the saved session is identical: live miles, accumulations, odometer prompt). The completion alert says "Your shift was closed with it — the tracker is stopped." The Oct 14 law is untouched: NO clock/dispatch-end-time path stops a session — this stop is the driver's own tap.
- FIXED (Suggestion Use vs busy form): Use is no longer a dead end when the form holds an edit/unsaved entry. It saves the suggestion as a SCHEDULED dispatch for its own day through the verified dspSaveRecord (never started, form/draft/active untouched), consumes the suggestion only after the save verifies, and explains itself at the tap ("Your form below was left exactly as it is — …"). DispatchIngest.use's refusal contract is unchanged; the module routes around it. Also fixed: the tap flash now renders when the LAST suggestion is consumed (it used to live only in the non-empty branch of the card).
- FIXED (stale "Tomorrow 8:00am"): suggestion cards derive the day word at RENDER time from the suggestion's date (dspRelDay: Today/Tomorrow/Yesterday/dated) + the clock (dspClockLabel). The ingest-baked timeLabel survives only as the no-date fallback.
- SHIPPED (item 3b, load capture v1): hourly/miles tracker gets a big "+1 load · N today" button (session accumulator manualLoads -> session record at stop); the close-with-ticket confirm takes "Loads hauled today" (pre-filled from the day's taps; blank stays unrecorded); ticket counts total onto the closed dispatch record (dspBuildClosed); the manifest prints a plain "Loads" line (new section toggle) with priority tickets > taps > weight-day counts — never summed, and the dispatch record's form-default loads ('1') is deliberately NOT a source. The manifest's only loads line before was the GPS cross-check, which cannot run when the dump site is given out loud (the normal case) — hence Loads 0 on a full hauling day. Geofence auto-count remains the designed follow-up (the v50/v68 GPS counter + dump-site learning already ship as that cross-check).
- Tests: NEW tools/dispatch-shiftclose-test.js 46/46; boss-firstrun (d2) updated to the v82 busy-Use contract 143/143; manifest-toggles updated for the 11th section 46/46. Full suite green except the two pre-existing wall-clock suites, identical to baseline.

## v83 — "Manifest times are HIS, not the app's" (Oct 15, shipped)
- FIXED (Manifest times): Start/End no longer default to the tracker session's stamps. collect() now resolves the day's dispatch records by scheduledAt-date (a dispatch saved the evening before FOR today is today's) and applies confirmed sources in priority: START = ticket start clock -> dispatch scheduled start -> started stamp; END (closed days) = ticket finish clock -> card finish DERIVED as scheduled start + the tickets' foreman hours (the card's handwritten finish is never OCR-read; 8:00 + 10.5h = 6:30 PM for card #21314) -> the record's endedAt (his own Close tap). Confirmed times print minute-clean ("8:00 AM", "6:30 PM" — no seconds). Session times survive only as a fallback and are LABELLED in preview + PDF ("Start time is from the tracker — no confirmed dispatch time for this day."). A still-running day keeps "Still working" for the end. The same day-record matching also feeds the v82 loads (tickets on an evening-before dispatch now count).

Not chased, per the brief.
- Tests: NEW tools/manifest-times-test.js 19/19 (the exact Oct 15 dataset derives 8:00 AM / 6:30 PM; ticket clocks win; close-tap fallback; tracker-only day labelled; live day keeps dispatch start + open end). Full suite green except the two pre-existing wall-clock suites, identical to baseline.

## v86 — "Every master write passes the same gate; the close takes a typed ticket; the time can be typed" (Oct 15, shipped)
- FIXED (Fuel-form odometer write — the CRITICAL live find): the write side of the odometer was never two-sided. The Fuel save fed the typed number into App._bumpOdometer (upward-only: any larger number sets), Maintenance's Update-odometer Save wrote W('odo', v) directly (any value, either direction), onboarding's finish set mt_odo from the baseline unchecked, and App._odoSaveDecision itself waved through any at-or-above value — its jump side simply didn't exist. v86: (1) odoSaveDecision gains the jump gate — a value more than +1,000 km above the master keeps unarmed and corrects only with the "This corrects a wrong odometer reading" arm, mirroring the below case; exactly +1,000 still sets; (2) bumpOdometer refuses jumps (sparse refused:'jump' key) so every indirect caller (day start/end sheets, reminder mirrors) is covered; (3) the Fuel form gains the correction checkbox (shown live only when the typed value trips the gate, worded per direction) and its save computes the decision BEFORE building the record — a refused jump saves the fill WITHOUT an odometer reading (an edit keeps its previous reading) with the refusal said out loud in the save note; an armed correction writes through with a "corrected" note; (4) Maintenance's Save routes through the decision with the same checkbox + direction-worded alerts; (5) Preferences paintFix + keep-note handle the jump direction; (6) onboarding's baseline write routes through the decision unarmed (first reading unaffected). Session ENTRIES still record the driver's typed readings as before — only the master is gated.
- MANIFEST LOADS (the note-with-no-number report): traced end-to-end with the REAL tracker functions + REAL dispatch render + REAL manifest — the chain is intact: the tap lands in trackerSave()'s accum, takeSessionAccum carries manualLoads onto the stopped session record, App.stopSessionOnDispatchClose fires inside applyCompletion, and collect() reads it back under the tickets > taps > weight priority (the record's form-default Loads '1' is correctly never a source). Both renderers already paired the count line with the source note in ONE guarded block, so the note cannot print numberless from this code — the live reading most plausibly came from the note line being read apart from the count line above it (preview) or split across a PDF column break. Hardening shipped anyway: the note now CARRIES the count in both renderers ("Counted with the +1 load button while working — 2 loads."), so annotation and number can never be seen apart; the replication test pins count + note together in collect() and the real preview HTML.
- FIXED (Close-with-ticket demanded a photo): the capture step offered camera/file or nothing. It now also offers "✍️ Type it instead — no photo", which opens the SAME confirm view with a hand ticket (manual: true, no photo); the confirm gains an editable Ticket / card # field on every ticket type (it was display-only — a misread number couldn't be fixed either), read back by currentCloseTicket (a typed number wins; an empty field never erases a read one). The hand close files the ticket, completes the dispatch and logs the foreman + travel money identically to a photo close (replication: #21314 + 10.5h → $333.50).
- The $0.24 "Auto from tracker" entry beside it is INTENDED, not a defect: revenue.onSession is the tracker's standing feed (v41) — every stopped session logs its tracked quantity, which is the only earnings record a tracker-only day has. Plain Complete doesn't create it (the session STOP does, any stop). With v81 grouping, a day carrying a confirmed completion counts the completion only — the auto entry is superseded in Revenue/invoice totals, verified on the exact live shape (confirmed 11.5h/$333.50 + a 0.01h auto entry → day and month totals $333.50).
- FIXED (Dispatch time could not be typed): the Scheduled field was a single <input type="datetime-local">, which accepts a typed time only as part of a complete date+time and silently discards a time typed alone — a human-facing defect, not automation-only; that's how the live dispatch got saved with no scheduled time at all. The field is now a date input + a time input (each typable AND pickable); dspComposeSched/dspSplitSched convert both ways and the stored format is unchanged ('YYYY-MM-DDTHH:MM', or the date alone). A time typed alone saves as TODAY at that time. Draft collect/restore, the active-note painter, the OCR prefill and readForm all use the pair. boss-firstrun's form assertion updated to the pair (v86).
- MANIFEST TIMES on the no-ticket day — verified, not changed: with a scheduled 08:00 the manifest Start IS 8:00 AM from the dispatch (priority fires; replication). The live dispatch carried no scheduledAt AND no startedAt; in that shape collect()'s start falls back to the tracker and the "from the tracker" label IS rendered in both preview and PDF lines (present since v83) — replication pins it. The live End "missing" is the same view mid-session (End shows the close stamp once the day is closed; replication pins it present after Complete).
- COMPLETION FEEDBACK — exists; verified end-to-end, no code change: applyCompletion's alert DOES append "Your shift was closed with it — the tracker is stopped." whenever stopSessionOnDispatchClose reports a stop, and the replication (real chain, captured alerts) fires it verbatim; without a session the sentence is correctly absent. The live driver missed it because browser automation auto-dismisses native alert() dialogs — the text never reaches a page snapshot.
- Tests: NEW tools/live-drive-test.js 68/68 (D1 decision/bump units; the fuel drive on the owner's exact numbers — refused unarmed: master stays 168,759, fill saved with no reading, refusal shown; armed from the fuel form: master corrects to 519,326; D2 chain: taps→session→Complete→collect+preview show 2; D5 alert verbatim + negative; D4b scheduled-priority + labelled-fallback; D3 manual close end-to-end + grouped totals on the live shape; D4a time-typed-alone + draft restore). odo-guard extended A27–A32 (73/73). boss-firstrun 144/144, maint-odo-photo 47/47 (its save-handler assertion updated to the v86 contract — the handler it pinned was the bug). Full suite: 35 suites, only the two pre-existing wall-clock failures remain, name- AND value-identical on the clean v85 baseline.

## Oct 15, 2026 (Asjad, 21:15 MDT) — DESIGN CORRECTION on load counting
- His words: "I told you yesterday that dump coordinates are usually not available on dispatch... They are verbally relayed to us on site!" ## Oct 16, 2026 (overnight soak, v88) — RECEIPT DATE FABRICATED FROM A CARD NUMBER'S TAIL
- Found by the soak's rotated real-data driver: the owner's Oct 14 fuel receipt with its lines reordered (date line last — a plausible OCR linearization) parsed its date as **2014-10-01**. Root cause: normDate's "24-Apr-18" pattern had NO word boundaries — in "VISA ****8001\nOct 14, 2026" it matched the tail "01" of 8001 as the day, "Oct" as the month, "14" as a 2-digit year, and that fabrication runs BEFORE the real "Oct 14, 2026" pattern. A fill-up landed on 2014 would vanish from its real day. FIX (v88): \b anchors on both ends of the day-month-year and slash-date patterns in normDate (shared by every parser). Legit shapes (14-Oct-26, 24-Apr-18, 10/14/2026) pinned still parsing. Tests: NEW tools/soak-regressions-test.js 14/14 (the failing permutations + traps in isolation + legitimate shapes). Full suite 2,273/2,274 — only the known f1 wall-clock.

## Oct 16, 2026 (overnight soak, v89) — THE DATE FABRICATION HAD A SECOND HEAD
- Pass 9's new seed shuffle put "TOTAL $234.32" on the line directly above "Oct 14, 2026" and the date came back MISSING. The cents "32" (a legit word boundary after the decimal point, so v88's \b could not stop it) posed as pattern 1's day across the newline; isoDate(2014,10,32) = null and normDate RETURNED the null, vetoing the real date in pattern 2. With cents <= 31 the same shape fabricated a valid wrong date (2015-10-14). FIX (v89): pattern 1 and pattern 2 separators are same-line only (hyphen/space/tab — the genuine shapes "24-Apr-18" / "Apr 24, 2018" never span lines), and a matched-but-invalid candidate now falls through to the remaining patterns instead of returning null. Regressions: soak-regressions 18/18 (both heads + seed-9's exact order + legitimate same-line shapes). Adjudicated NON-defect in the same pass: TicketOCR.parse is the dispatch photo-intake classifier (fuel receipts never flow through it — their door is parseFuelReceipt, pinned separately).

## FEATURE — YARD-DEPARTURE ACTIVATION (Asjad, Oct 16, 2026 07:15 MDT)
His words: "every time a user leaves their yard… the Dispatch saved in their dispatch module should be turned on automatically." Born from this morning's adhkar watch (GPS departure → action). SPEC SKETCH: the yard = the user's base place (home for owner-ops who park at home — his case; a settable "Yard" personal place). When the phone leaves the yard radius on a morning with a saved dispatch for that day, the app ACTIVATES that dispatch (status live, today's plan armed) without the driver opening the app — same behind-the-glass family as dump-site learning (one geofence engine: yard departure + pit/dump arrival learning). Composes with the existing dispatch-driven tracker auto-start (dispatch time, 8:00 Mon–Sat fallback, backdate-on-wake). Boundaries to respect when built: pay stays ticket-based (activation ≠ pay start); the session still ends only when HE stops it; a departure with NO saved dispatch that day does nothing (no phantom days); radius generous enough that a trip to the store doesn't arm a workday — arm only when a same-day dispatch exists.

## Oct 16, 2026 (v92 / native v35) — BACKGROUND KILOMETRES: THE DAY COUNTS WITHOUT THE APP
Owner's restated demand (Oct 16, 08:12 MDT, after Oct 14's ~13.7/100 km and Oct 15's 62.4/177 km undercounts and his Oct 16 confirmation that the counter "only came alive with the 8:00 session"): the day's kilometres must be counted FROM THE BACKGROUND — screen off, app closed, phone in pocket — including the pre-session drive.
- DIAGNOSIS (why v34's service wasn't enough): the foreground service + breadcrumb store were sound, but two structural holes remained. (1) THE SERVICE COULD ONLY START FROM THE WEBVIEW — a session begins in JavaScript (manual tap or the app.js auto-start), so a phone whose app was dead at 8:00 recorded nothing until the driver opened the app, and if Samsung killed the service mid-shift nothing anywhere tried to revive it. (2) THE DRAIN DROPPED PRE-SESSION CRUMBS — shifttrack treated every crumb older than (session start − 60 s) as a stale leftover: cleared, never folded. The morning's drive from home was observed by nobody, by construction.
- THE MECHANISM (native v35, `meticulous-native`): THE ARMED DAY. While the app is alive, js/app.js `App.armNativeDay()` computes the next planned start from the SAME autostart engine the tracker runs on (per-day override > today's/tomorrow's dispatch > standing setting; gated by the standing on/off + days + driver role) and hands it to the shell via the new ShiftTrack plugin methods armDay/disarmDay: {sessionKey 's<startMs>' — the same key an anchored auto-start pins — fireMs = startMs − 60 min, startMs, date}. The 60-minute lead is deliberate: it is exactly LiveMiles' own pre-session adoption slack (ADOPT_FIRST_SLACK_MS), so the window the arm opens is the window the engines already treat as belonging to the day. At fireMs, DayAlarmReceiver → DayScheduler.tryStartFromArm persists the session in SharedPreferences and starts ShiftTrackingService itself — no WebView in the loop. Alarm tiers, strongest first, reported in status as alarmTier: exact (setExactAndAllowWhileIdle when canScheduleExactAlarms — USE_EXACT_ALARM ships in the manifest, SCHEDULE_EXACT_ALARM also declared) → alarm-clock (setAlarmClock) → inexact. Guards: a stale arm (>6 h past its planned start) is consumed silently; an arm never hijacks an already-recording session; a refused background start rolls the session state back and records 'day-start-refused'. Recomputed on boot / resume / session start / session stop / plan changes; re-arming the same arm is a native no-op.
- THE WATCHDOG (native): a chained allow-while-idle alarm (15 min) runs while a session records or an arm is pending. A recording silent >12 min gets a service-restart attempt; refusals are counted (watchdogRefusals) and recorded ('watchdog-restart-refused'). The service schedules the chain on every start and pokes a 1-minute shot from onTaskRemoved; plugin stop() cancels the chain but re-arms a pending day-arm's fire-time backstop. BootReceiver now also restores a pending arm after a reboot (re-schedules a future alarm, fires a due one).
- THE WEB HALVES: (a) the drain's freshness cutoff is now the day's true window — the earliest of session start − 1 min, the stored dayarm's fire time, and the native startedAt for the key — so armed-morning crumbs FOLD (live engine when the session is up, stored record when it isn't); crumbs older than the window are still cleared unfolded. (b) foldIntoRecord CREATES the day's record when none exists — natively observed miles are never acked into the void; the record lands under the arm's key, which is the session's own key, and LiveMiles.startDay resumes from it when the session begins. (c) syncOnBoot refreshes the stored window from the plugin's status before draining. (d) One accumulator, unchanged: screen number, manifest and revenue still read LiveMiles; the engines' honesty rules are untouched (a true observer hole is still counted only at its straight-line floor and REPORTED by the gap finder — never guessed up).
- BATTERY HONESTY: the tracker card's once-per-session ask (existing bannerDone mechanism) now covers the battery state: batteryExempt=false, or a recorded service refusal (foreground-service-refused / day-start-refused / watchdog-restart-refused), shows ONE plain line — "Battery saver can stop your miles mid-shift. Set Meticulous battery to 'Unrestricted'…" — with an Open-battery-settings button; settled for the session when answered. Preferences → Advanced → Background tracking additionally states the armed day in plain words ("Next shift: counting starts by itself tomorrow at 7:00 AM — no need to open the app first" / "Today's counting started by itself"). The direct battery-exemption dialog's permission (REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS) is now actually declared in the manifest.
- FOREGROUND: unchanged — timer, wake hold, screen counting, pause/stop, +1 load, dispatch close stopping the session, and the Oct 16 8:00 anchored auto-start all behave as before; native-track-test (35/35) and dispatch-autostart-test (58/58) pin them. Sessions still end only when HE stops them (plugin stop disarms nothing about tomorrow; the watchdog never stops anything).
- TESTS: NEW tools/background-day-test.js 39/39. (A) The real miles engine fed ONLY a synthetic native-crumb stream (screen never opened; pre-session leg + session legs + an ~11-min observer gap with observed endpoints + a 3-min-rationed stretch + bad-accuracy junk): 47.50 km counted vs 47.95 km ground truth = 99.1%, re-feed adds 0, gap still reported (12.31 km). (B) A true 20 km unobserved hard-bend detour: counted 22.23 of 27.97 km — the straight-line floor only, gap named (14.73 km), never guessed up. (C) The real shifttrack glue in a vm against a fake v35 plugin: armed morning with an idle tracker creates the record and folds the full 10.00 km window (got 9.99 km); unarmed day keeps the exact v91 cutoff (5.49 of 10 km); no ack while the live engine is unready; battery + refused-service lines fire once with their fix; pre-v35 shell stays inert. (D) Real AUTOSTART-ENGINE + armNativeDay on a fake clock: tomorrow's 07:30 dispatch arms tomorrow at 06:30 under 's<dispatch-start-ms>'; standing-time and mid-session cases arm tomorrow; auto-start-off and office role disarm. (E) Statics pin the native sources, manifest, and version agreement (web 92 / APK_VERSION 35 / versionCode 10 / versionName 1.8 / pins ?v=92). FULL SUITE: 2,374 passed, 1 failed — only the known manifest-export f1 wall-clock, name-identical to the pre-round baseline. Native Java: parsed clean against android.jar (javac) — every residual error is a missing androidx/Capacitor symbol the Gradle build supplies.
- BUILD STATE (honest): the APK was NOT produced in this environment. Gradle still dies with "Could not receive a message from the daemon" (the sandbox's JVM localhost-socket block, exactly as NATIVE-SETUP.md documents), and this shell has no GitHub credentials (gh unauthenticated; git push cannot read a username), so the Actions build cannot be triggered from here either. Both repos are committed LOCALLY, release-ready: meticulous-standalone at web v92, meticulous-native with the v35 sources + a fully re-synced www (the whole v92 web set — the bundled fallback had drifted several rounds behind) + versionCode 10 / 1.8. TO SHIP: push meticulous-native main → Actions builds the signed v35 APK (fixed release keystore) → put its URL in version.json apkUrl → push meticulous-app main (deploys web v92 + the banner). Until the v35 APK is installed, his v34 shell has no armDay: the web side detects that and behaves exactly like v91 (counting starts when the app wakes) — and even on v35, the day is only as safe as his battery setting: if Meticulous is not Unrestricted, Samsung can still kill the service and refuse the watchdog's restart; the app now SAYS so, once, instead of failing silently. PROOF STANDARD (owner's): one full workday, live miles vs his odometer — unproven until that day runs.
- SHIPPED (Oct 16, 2026, ship run after the build round): both repos pushed via the Git Data API helper (gh-push-api.py, needs PYTHONPATH=/opt/muse/skills/custom-connector-setup/bin). meticulous-native main -> a4905cd; Actions run 38917310911 "Build signed release APK" SUCCESS; artifact meticulous-release-apk (8,549,399-byte app-release.apk, sha256 cd86f763daa6837c7c0e2e0e510715bb5e802a172e14addb26843f33256ba330; bundled version.json inside reads version 35 / webVersion 92 and the dex carries DayScheduler/DayAlarmReceiver/WatchdogReceiver/armDay). meticulous-app main -> 799c491; Deploy run 38917347267 SUCCESS; live version.json verified webVersion 92 / version 35 and the five v92-changed served files hash-match the manifest exactly.
- APK HOSTING LESSON (cost one failed deploy): Firebase Hosting on the Spark plan REJECTS executable files outright ("Executable files are forbidden on the Spark billing plan", deploy run 38918300682 failed at populateFiles; the live site was untouched). The v35 APK therefore does NOT live on meticulous-dd570.web.app. It is served from the app repo's GitHub Pages (gh-pages branch, commit 3f72f66, Pages was already enabled): https://asjadkam.github.io/Meticulous-app/meticulous-v35.apk — verified HTTP 200 with bytes sha256-identical to the Actions artifact. version.json apkUrl points there (app main 91bc6cf; Deploy run 38918814566 SUCCESS; live version.json shows version 35 / webVersion 92 / that apkUrl; live index pins are ?v=92 only). The APK file itself was removed from the app main tree — leaving it there would break every future Firebase deploy the same way. A copy also sits at ~/workspace/your_files/meticulous-v35.apk for the usual manual send. NOTE for future rounds: drop each new APK onto the gh-pages branch and bump apkUrl; never commit an .apk to main again. The native repo is PRIVATE, so a release asset there could never serve as the public apkUrl; release-asset upload also needs uploads.github.com, outside the legacy connector's api.github.com-only rule.
- MANIFEST FOOTNOTE: the local committed version.json lists eng.traineddata (5.2 MB) but that file is UNTRACKED in this repo (never committed), so CI's regenerated live manifest (40 entries) omits it and Hosting answers its URL with the SPA rewrite — unchanged from v91 and every earlier round; not a v92 regression.
- STILL TRUE after shipping: v92's proof standard is unmet until one full workday on the installed v35 APK matches his odometer, and battery Unrestricted on his phone remains the #1 starvation risk. The phone-side step left: install v35 (in-app banner now offers it, or the your_files copy) — until then his v34 shell runs web v92 in v91-behavior mode by design.

## Oct 16, 2026 (v93) — THE ROAD HAND: TRUCKING CHATBOT v1 + TURN-BY-TURN HANDOFF
Owner's top priority today (Oct 16, 09:21 MDT: "Today primarily you're supposed to be focusing on the chatbot I explained yesterday"), plus his same-morning directive (09:25 MDT) that turn-by-turn must NOT wait for the routing server.
- SHIPPED — THE ASK BUTTON (js/chatbot.js + css): ONE persistent round mic-first button (🎤), bottom-right on every main-app screen, stacked above the feedback FAB (bottom:88px) so it never covers the home tracker or a primary action; visibility mirrors the feedback FAB's main-view rule via MutationObserver. Tap → bottom sheet (fbSheet pattern): the spec's greeting (specific → broad examples), then THREE tappable example chips rotating through a pool of 8 across opens (offset persisted). A tapped slide example does NOT answer with the example's numbers — it opens the weights conversation for the driver's own (the spec's follow-up rule). Voice in: platform SpeechRecognition (en-CA) when the device offers it, with an honest typed fallback line when it doesn't; text input always present. Voice out: the Navigator's in-house speechSynthesis path — answers auto-speak ONLY when the question was asked by voice; every bot card carries its own 🔊 tap that speaks that card even when the sheet is toggled quiet (the tap is the request). Sound toggle in the sheet header, persisted in the synced 'chat' key.
- LAYER 1 — DETERMINISTIC TRUCK MATH (CHAT-CORE, pure, never guessed): axle-slide weight shifting from statics (per-hole transfer = group weight × hole spacing ÷ span): trailer tandems (slide BACK toward the rear doors loads the drives; FORWARD toward the cab loads the trailer), fifth wheel (FORWARD loads the steer), and shifting the load itself in a dump box (per-foot lever). Defaults are TYPICAL geometry (40 ft kingpin-to-tandem, 4-in holes, 200-in steer-to-drive span, 2-in notches) and are STATED in every answer so the driver can correct them; his own per-hole figure ("300 a hole") wins outright. The conversation asks ONE question at a time (which group → how much over) and derives the heavy group + excess from full scale weights when given. After-slide legality is checked against the receiving group — a slide that would push the other group over its Alberta limit is called out ("Sliding alone can't fix this one"). Alberta legal weights from Alberta Transportation's own summaries (retrieved Oct 16, 2026): steer 7,300 kg; single 9,100 (duals/wide-base) / 7,300 (single tires); tandem 17,000 / 13,600; tridem 21,000 (spread 2.4–<3.0 m) / 24,000 (≥3.0 m); GVW capped at the sum of legal axle weights. A tridem spread under 2.4 m is NOT in the verified table — the bot says it doesn't have that row instead of inventing one. The US bridge formula ships as a clearly-labelled sanity check only (Alberta has no bridge law). Unit handling: explicit words win; otherwise judged from the groups themselves (a tandem-class value >26,000 can only be pounds) — the summed gross is deliberately excluded from that guess after a test caught it reading a 39,900 kg gross as pounds.
- LAYER 2 — CALGARY LEGALITY: "is <street> legal for my truck" answered from the conflated dataset (truck-nav: City of Calgary DG Truck Routes mt_* tags on OSM, 92.5% conflation), bundled as js/chat-routes.json — 11,576 named streets, 398 KB, geometry dropped, fetched lazily on the first legality question only (generator: tools/gen-chat-routes.js). Answers compose from the street's own data: designated share (all / parts / a sliver / none), route types, printed restrictions with the overnight window evaluated against the clock ("that window is on RIGHT NOW"), a conflict flag where the city list and the road data disagree, and the standing humility lines — not-on-the-network keeps the shortest-local-in-and-out door open, and "Posted signs govern" closes every answer. Unknown streets are NOT invented: the bot says it doesn't have the name and offers close matches. Address-level precision it does not claim: the index is per-street, so a long street with mixed stretches gets the "parts of" answer, never a blanket yes.
- LAYER 3 — LEG WEATHER (Open-Meteo, free, no key): "weather at my drop" resolves today's dispatch (its saved pin when the record carries one, else a name geocode), "weather in <place>" tries the app's own pinned built-in places first, then Open-Meteo geocoding (CA-biased); bare "weather" uses the last GPS fix / a one-shot fix. 10-minute cache; every failure mode answers honestly (couldn't pin the place, service didn't answer) — never a fabricated forecast.
- LAYER 4 — THE APP'S OWN RECORDS (READ-ONLY; this module writes no record, ever): today's dispatch (job, clock, material, drop, ticket, status), loads today (the manifest's own priority mirrored: ticket counts > +1 taps — including a still-running day's live taps — > Weight-day counts), hours today and period gross from Meticulous.shared.buildRevenueLines (the SAME grouped builder the invoice prints from, so a completion + tracker day is never double-counted here either; raw-entry fallback only when the builder somehow isn't loaded, and it FLAGS hours-only entries it couldn't price), master odometer. Semi-monthly period boundaries match the invoice cycle.
- LAYER 5 — GENERAL QUESTIONS, NO KEY ERRAND (owner correction Oct 16, 08:19 MDT, applied to this feature from birth): the bot routes through Power Read's cloud seam with ZERO key UI of its own — no field, no prompt, no nag (pinned in tests). Transport order: a future server proxy (store key chatProxyUrl, set by the server ship — deliberately NO UI in this feature) → the owner's own pre-existing Power Read setup, used silently when present. When no brain is reachable: layers 1–4 answer where they fit; anything else gets ONE honest line ("That one needs my general brain, and it isn't online yet — it comes with the server, and I won't guess at it…"). NOTHING is queued, stored, or promised behind that line (pinned). Remote answers render under a "General information" tag, per the guardrail that legal/safety numbers come from layers 1–2 or are labelled.
- NOT IN v1 (plainly): traffic prediction ("what's traffic like on Deerfoot at 5" routes to the general layer and gets the honest line until the server brain lands), the server-side general brain itself (Oracle signup is mid-flight after the card-decline halt), contextual "Ask about this dispatch" entry points (spec Phase 2), and any persistence of conversations (state is sheet-scoped; the weights conversation survives interjected questions but not a sheet close).
- SHIPPED — TURN-BY-TURN HANDOFF (modules/navigator.js, owner Oct 16 09:25 MDT): when a destination is set (search, map tap, saved-place picker, or the v87 nav_pending hand-off — all land on the same setDest), a "▶ Turn-by-turn" action appears with ONE honest line under it: "Opens Google Maps for spoken directions. Meticulous's own drive screen is coming." It hands the destination PIN + name to the phone's maps app: Google Maps navigation intent first (package-addressed, its browser_fallback_url carrying the web-directions URL), then a timed cascade while the page is still in front — geo: URI (any maps app) → Waze → Google Maps web directions; a page-hide cancels the rest. Primary/secondary order follows the server state: NO server → the handoff is THE primary action and Route keeps its exact server-not-set behaviour below it; server set → Route leads, the handoff sits under it as a quiet ghost. The destination note now names the right tap for the state. No destination → the button stays hidden; a tap in that state (reachable only before first render completes) explains itself and opens nothing. The in-app Route flow, its warning card, and its server-not-set behaviour are byte-for-byte as before. HONEST LIMIT: spoken turn-by-turn works TODAY via this handoff; the in-app 3D heading-up drive screen still awaits the routing server (unchanged plan). On-device cascade behaviour (esp. the stock shell's intent handling) is verified by construction + harness, not yet by a road test on the owner's phone.
- Tests: NEW tools/chatbot-test.js 128/128 (hand-computed slide pins incl. the spec's 800 lb example = 3 holes; Alberta table + bridge caps; the one-question-at-a-time conversation incl. two caught-and-fixed defects — gross-poisoned unit guessing, word-first parse stealing the next group's number — and one fixed TEST case where the bot was right; legality on the REAL index incl. 14 Street NW's overnight window on/off by the clock; intent routing table incl. the spec's absurd off-topic question → general; records fixtures incl. ticket-loads-beats-taps and grouped-builder gross; no-key/no-queue honesty statics). navigator-test EXTENDED 70/70 (pure ttnLinks: intent/geo/Waze/web from the set destination; render order both server states; hidden-until-destination; the four-step cascade fired in order against captured timers; no-destination tap opens nothing). background-day-test E8/E10 pins moved to web 93 (native stays 35, apkUrl untouched). FULL SUITE: 40 suites, 2,502+ checks — only the known manifest-export f1 wall-clock failure remains, name-identical to the pre-round baseline.

Each read costs a fraction of a cent.'" The wiring IIFE no longer reads or writes any key (PowerRead.setKey has no UI caller left). Nothing to set up — and the answer always waits for your check." + the "Power read on" toggle (behaviour unchanged — it still gates escalation; OFF = on-device only) + a quiet note (month's read count; queued reads waiting for signal).

## Oct 16, 2026 (v95) — LOCAL MODE GETS A SIGN-IN DOOR; ADVANCED GETS A DRAWER
Two owner rulings from the Oct 16 field screenshots. (1) Muse's browser copy of the app sat in Local mode with NO reachable sign-in: the only route was Preferences → Advanced → "Reset onboarding questions" — a destructive-sounding detour whose handler fires a native confirm() that browser automation auto-cancels, and which a human reads as "reset your setup to log in." His law: if the user has to look twice, it's broken. (2) On the same Preferences screenshot: "Put the entire advanced drawer in a button somewhere, not in the wide open like that."
- THE DOOR (index.html + js/app.js + modules/preferences.js): ONE primary-styled "Sign in" button in the two places a signed-out user looks. (a) THE DRAWER: #sbSignIn in the sidebar account block, under the name/email lines; it starts hidden in the markup and App.onAuth paints it with the exact same funnel that paints sbEmail/sbName — visible iff there is no signed-in user, so it can never appear mid-session for a signed-in user or disagree with the "Local mode / (offline)" lines beside it. Its click closes the drawer and calls the door router. (b) PREFERENCES: #pf_signin at the TOP of the Security card in the signed-out render (replacing the dead-end note "Sign in to change your password." — a sign-in instruction with no door attached), with one plain note (Local mode = data lives only here; sign in to back up, sync, change password). The Security card stays the LAST card and outside Advanced (below), so the door is visible the moment Preferences opens.
- THE ROUTER (js/app.js, App.goSignIn, defined in wireAuth beside showAuthForm): showDashboard() first (entering from a module returns #main to the dashboard, so a completed sign-in — or a skip back — lands exactly where the boot path lands, never on a stale module render; it also runs the module teardown), then showAuthForm('signin') + App.showView('auth'). It touches NOTHING else: no store write, no onboarding flag, no local-data change (pinned by test). A completed sign-in continues through the ordinary afterAuth() path — the existing post-sign-in merge/sync, unmodified. The way back without signing in is the auth form's own existing "Continue without an account" (enterLocalMode → afterAuth → main for an onboarded local user). Reset onboarding questions keeps its own confirm + purpose, byte-identical, for users who actually want the questions again.
- THE DRAWER (modules/preferences.js): the Advanced card's open stack is replaced by ONE quiet ghost button (#pf_adv_toggle, "⚙️ Advanced ▸", aria-expanded) and a container (#pf_adv_body) that renders with class hidden — CLOSED every time Preferences opens. Opening it reveals exactly the pre-v95 contents, unchanged: Check for updates, Notifications, Cloud sync troubleshooting, Power read, Background tracking, Park-place nudges, Navigator server, Reset onboarding fenced last in danger colours — same controls, ids, order, wiring and v94 copy (the toggle handler flips only the container + caret + aria state). All the row wiring queries the same ids (elements inside a hidden container are still in the DOM), so status paints and handlers behave identically whether the drawer is open or shut. Render smoke (both auth states): div balance 24/24, container closed by default, every Advanced control inside the container span, the sign-in door inside the Security card after it.
- Tests: role-package 119→161 (NEW v95 statics — Preferences: door exactly once, primary, built with the Security card, NOT inside the drawer markup, wired to App.goSignIn, dead-end note gone; drawer: toggle + container exactly once each, container hidden by default, old open header gone, all nine Advanced control ids survive exactly once and render inside the container, Security appended after Reset, toggle wiring flips hidden + aria-expanded, Reset's confirm intact. App/index: drawer door exactly once, primary + hidden in markup, inside the account block; App.goSignIn exists, routes via showAuthForm('signin') + showView('auth'), resets to the dashboard first, and its body contains no store/onboarded/localStorage touch; App.onAuth paints it iff !user; the auth form's Continue-without-an-account back-path intact). background-day E8/E10 + chatbot H2 version pins moved 94→95 (native stays 35, apkUrl untouched). ## Oct 16, 2026 (v96) — ROAD HAND GETS A REAL TEXT BOX; MUSH GETS A SHORT ANSWER
Two fixes from the owner's first live Road Hand field test (Oct 16, screen recording). (1) He asked a question by voice, it arrived as "HowwoH", and got the long general-brain fallback speech. (2) His verdict on the composer: "how do you send it any messages? There should be a text box as well."
- THE TEXT BOX (js/chatbot.js + css/style.css): the composer always HAD an input, but it rendered as a thin unlabelled pill at the row's left edge — invisible at a glance. ROOT CAUSE in CSS: .btn is width:100% app-wide, so the Ask button's flex base size was the whole row and flex-shrink took the overflow out of the input (flex base 0), leaving it at min-width:0 + padding — a sliver. The mic and the navy Ask button owned the row; nothing on screen said "type here." FIX: the input is now a real text input (type="text", class chat-in, enterkeyhint="send") with the plain placeholder "Type a question…", and row-scoped CSS makes the field the row's primary element (.chat-row .chat-in{flex:1 1 auto;width:auto} — it takes every spare pixel and escapes the global input width:100%) while the send button goes compact (.chat-row .btn{flex:none;width:auto}). Row order unchanged: field, mic, Ask. Voice is untouched; typed text sends through the SAME ask() as voice — the Ask button and Enter both call ask(value, {viaVoice:false}), the mic result calls ask(transcript, {viaVoice:true}). Nothing else in the panel moved: intro card, chips, answer cards, speaker toggles byte-identical.
- THE GARBLE GATE (CHAT-CORE isGarble + GARBLE_LINE): input with no recognizable words is not a general question and no longer earns the general-brain speech. The detector is conservative: a message is garble only when NONE of its alpha tokens grips — a token grips if it is in a built-in vocabulary (the intents' own words, street suffixes, plain-English function words, common general-question words) or is within edit distance 1 of a longer vocabulary word, so one-edit typos of real terms ("wheather", "hols", "drivs") clear the gate and flow to their proper layer. Empty and numbers-only input are never garble (a bare "800" mid-conversation still belongs to the weights talk, which absorbs general-layer follow-ups before the gate). In ask() the gate sits after every deterministic layer and the pending-math absorption, immediately before the general seam: garble → ONE short spoken line, "Didn't catch that — say it again, or tap one of these." + the example chips re-offered (renderChips, the same renderer openSheet uses — chipsHtml rotation untouched). Genuine general questions ("who won the game") keep the seam + OFFLINE_LINE path verbatim; the proxy seam, TTS, and the chip pool are untouched.
- Tests: chatbot-test 128→161 (NEW Part I, the garble gate: field-test mush + keyboard mush are garble; one known word clears; "who won the game" and "what is the capital of France?" are NOT garble and still route general; garble routes general too — only the reply swaps; typo'd weather/holes/drives lines clear via the one-edit rule; bare numbers/empty/bare group answers never garble; the line is short, chip-pointing, and never the general-brain speech; glue pins: gate in ask() ahead of remoteAsk, short line spoken, chips re-offered. NEW Part J, the composer: real text input markup + "Type a question…" placeholder, old pill placeholder gone, CSS pins for the flexing field + compact button, mic door stays, voice/button/Enter all pinned to the one ask()). background-day E8/E10 + chatbot H2 version pins moved 95→96 (native stays 35, apkUrl untouched). FULL SUITE: 40 suites + OCR harness 32/32 — baseline captured before the round (40 pass / manifest-export f1 fail) and after: identical except chatbot 128→161; the only failure stays manifest-export f1, name-identical.
- SHIPPED (Oct 16, 2026): local commit 323cd63 pushed via the Git Data API helper -> remote 194efc5; Deploy workflow landed; live https://meticulous-dd570.web.app/version.json reads webVersion 96 / version 35 / apkUrl untouched (meticulous-v35.apk on GitHub Pages); the three v96-changed served files (js/chatbot.js, css/style.css, index.html) hash-match the live manifest exactly; live index pins are ?v=96 only; the live bytes carry the chat-in composer markup + "Type a question…" placeholder, the .chat-row .chat-in/.btn rules, isGarble and the rendered GARBLE_LINE. ## v97 queue — MASTER ODOMETER INFLATED BY LEG SUMS (found Oct 16, 2026, field report)
- Symptom: Maintenance card "⚠️ Oil change overdue"; app claims OVERDUE by 343,897 km. Verified live Oct 16 (~14:55 MDT, owner's account): app stored odometer = **519,326 km**; real dash = **168,940 km** (Oct 16 morning). Sticker target in app = 175,429 km / 2026-11-27 → real remaining = 6,489 km. Owner is right; the app's odo is corrupt.
- Smoking gun on screen: "Tracked over 350,816 km (2 legs)" and 519,326 − 350,816 = 168,510 ≈ true odo (168,518 Oct 14 start). A writer is ADDING tracked leg distances into the master odometer (mt_odo) on top of verified captures — bypassing the two-sided gate's intent.
- Fix: (1) find and kill the leg-sum writer — mt_odo may move ONLY via gated captures (dash photo / fuel / manual), never by GPS leg accumulation (legs feed Live Miles only); (2) ship a one-time verified correction path: owner photographs the dash, app sets mt_odo to the photo value (downward allowed only through this correction flow, logged); (3) regression: simulate legs + captures, assert mt_odo equals last capture exactly.
- Data state at find: no services ever logged; oil interval 8,000 km; countdown uses manual sticker values (175,429 / 2026-11-27) since no oil service logged.

## Oct 16, 2026 (v97) — MASTER ODOMETER: THE LEG-SUM THEORY, CORRECTED; THE CORRECTION DOOR OPENS
The v97-queue entry above (written from the owner's screen) inferred a writer ADDING GPS leg distances into mt_odo. The code census says otherwise — precisely:
- ROOT CAUSE (pre-v86 modules/fuel.js save + modules/maintenance.js readers): 519,326 was the INVOICE NUMBER on the Oct 15 fuel receipt (established by the v80 suite and the v86 commit). The pre-v86 fuel save carried it into the master AND saved it on the fill record. v86 gated every writer, so no current code can write it — but Maintenance's readers kept trusting recorded readings blindly: currentOdo() took the max over ALL fill/event odometers, and mileageStats() summed "legs" between consecutive fill odometers. The poisoned fill (519,326) beside the true 168,510 fill is exactly the 350,816 km "leg" on screen (519,326 − 168,510 = 350,816) — the arithmetic that looked like leg addition was the poisoned FILL record, not GPS legs. Writer census, pinned by the new suite: js/miles.js (Live Miles) and js/geoloads.js / js/shifttrack.js / js/geo.js / js/yardstart.js never reference mt_odo at all; every mt_odo write in the repo is a gated capture (app.js bumpOdometer body + onboarding decision site, preferences.js decision site, fuel.js's two armed 'correct' sites, maintenance.js's decision writes).
- THE READ GATE (modules/maintenance.js): new plausibleReading() — a recorded fill/event reading that App._odoImplausible judges a 'jump' (more than the master + ODO_MAX_JUMP_KM) is bad data and is ignored by currentOdo() and mileageStats(). With no master there is nothing to judge against and every reading passes, exactly as before. On the owner's data, once he corrects the master, the poisoned fill drops out of the math: displayed odometer = the master, "Tracked over" = the true 249 km leg (168,510 → 168,759), and the oil countdown returns to the sticker truth — 6,489 km remaining to 175,429, NOT overdue.
- THE CORRECTION DOOR (modules/maintenance.js): a clearly-marked card, "🛠️ Correct odometer from dash photo", directly under the dashboard card — its own field (mt_fix) with the standing two-button photo wiring (mt_fix_cam / mt_fix_pick; the camera input carries capture=environment), fill-only through the shared App._wirePhotoOCR pipeline exactly like the v61 card: no read path writes anything until "Save correction" is tapped. The save runs applyOdoCorrection() — the supervised decision (App._odoSaveDecision) ARMED — so the confirmed dash value may set mt_odo DOWNWARD, only through this flow; ordinary writes stay upward-only and jump-refused under the existing guard (pinned). Every actual change of value appends an audit entry to the service history (type 'odocorr', labelled "Odometer correction", date + odometer = new value + notes "Odometer corrected from X km to Y km (dash photo)", mirrored via FB.saveDoc like a service log); the existing Update-odometer Save's 'correct' outcomes are audited the same way (source 'typed reading'). Re-saving the same value logs nothing twice.
- OWNER DATA UNTOUCHED: his stored 519,326 stands until HE runs the new correction from his own dash photo (tonight's Oct 16 close). No back-end edit was made or will be.
- Tests: NEW tools/odo-correction-test.js 65/65 — A: the real app.js gate block (a dash capture lands exactly; a leg-sum-sized write is refused as a jump and the master does not move; ordinary writes cannot lower it; the armed decision corrects a poisoned master). B: the writer census above. C: the whole maintenance module over the field data reproduces the owner's screen exactly (350,816 km over 2 legs, displayed odo 519,326, oil overdue by 343,897 km), then the correction flow fixes it (master 168,940 exact; exactly one audit entry naming 519,326 → 168,940; tracked 249 km / 1 leg; oil remaining 6,489, not overdue; a repeat correction logs nothing; garbage is ignored). D: correction-card statics (marked card, both photo buttons, capture attribute, fill-only wiring, audit label). maint-odo-photo 47→48: its "exactly one W('odo', …)" pin became "exactly two, both decision values" — the v97 flow is the deliberate second supervised write, and the no-stray-write invariant is now asserted directly. FULL SUITE: 41 suites + OCR harness 32/32 — baseline captured before the round (40 pass / manifest-export f1 fail); after: identical plus the new suite, and the only failure stays manifest-export f1, name-identical to the v96 baseline.
- SHIPPED (Oct 16, 2026): local commit 1208043 pushed via the Git Data API helper -> remote e327a19; Deploy workflow landed; live https://meticulous-dd570.web.app/version.json reads webVersion 97 / version 35 / apkUrl untouched (meticulous-v35.apk on GitHub Pages); the two v97-changed served files hash-match the live manifest exactly (modules/maintenance.js ba1b1be8753932eec485f69ddc92b4c23ebecfd00d93f1ee452ea5959bb163e0, index.html fdad6f81f0bf0b2d258c866d6a21aca6cafa872c2504689d93b2bcc759c4ffef); the live maintenance.js bytes carry plausibleReading, the gated currentOdo/mileageStats, the ODO-CORRECTION region and the "🛠️ Correct odometer from dash photo" card; local index pin for maintenance.js is ?v=97 (proven by the hash match). ## Oct 16, 2026 (v98) — THE NAVIGATOR SHIPS WITH ITS SERVER: ROUTING WORKS OUT OF THE BOX
The routing server went live today (Hetzner CX23, Valhalla 3.9.1, production Let's Encrypt cert, /route + /status, CORS pinned to the app origin) and passed its end-to-end smoke test at https://185.31.47.19 — but the app still shipped with NO server address, so every install honestly reported "Navigator server not set — routing unavailable" until someone typed the address into Preferences → Advanced by hand. That was one errand too many (the take-load-off law): the address is now the app's DEFAULT.
- THE DEFAULT (modules/navigator.js): new NAV_DEFAULT_SERVER_URL = 'https://185.31.47.19', applied by loadSettings() ONLY when the store carries no serverUrl key at all (never saved). Semantics pinned by suite: never-saved → the default; a saved custom address always wins; a DELIBERATELY CLEARED address ('' saved by clearing the Preferences field) stays cleared — the default never resurrects over a saved value, and the honest unavailable states (warning card, status line, the v93 handoff as primary action) stay reachable exactly as before. Exposed as MeticulousNav.DEFAULT_SERVER_URL for Preferences/tests. (The nav.meticuloustechnologies.com name replaces this IP literal once its DNS lands — the zone sits on the old cPanel host, not Namecheap; see the Oct 16 deploy log.)
- PREFERENCES (modules/preferences.js): the Advanced field now shows the EFFECTIVE address (navLoad applies the same rule), so a fresh install displays the default, the note says "Server set", and clearing the field remains the one honest way to turn routing off. The module-load fallback in navLoad duplicates the literal on purpose (it exists for when navigator.js failed to load) and a navigator-test static pins both copies to the same value. No control, id, order or behaviour changed; role-package stays 161/161.
- v93 HANDOFF UNCHANGED: the cascade code is untouched; both presentations are pinned — primary when no server is effective (cleared), ghost under Route when one is (custom or the v98 default, where a tap still fires the Google Maps intent first).
- Tests: navigator-test 70 → 84 — the old "no server set" world is re-seeded as deliberately-cleared ('') with a new pin that a cleared server stays cleared; NEW "default server, never set" block (exposed default, loadSettings default, custom-wins, status line, no warning card, Route POSTs exactly https://185.31.47.19/route with the truck body, dest note + ghost ordering, handoff cascade first link); statics updated (no loopback/private literals; the one baked-in address is the shipped default; Preferences fallback carries the same rule). card-previews: the {} world now expects the profile line under the default + a new cleared-world eq ("Server not set — routing off"). ## Oct 16, 2026 (v99) — NAVIGATOR DEFAULT: THE ADDRESS WAS WRONG, CORRECTED

URGENT correction round, same day as v98. v98 baked the WRONG server address into the app default: NAV_DEFAULT_SERVER_URL = 'https://185.31.47.19'. That address is DEAD — it never answers, not even from the server itself.
- ROOT CAUSE: the 185.31.47.19 figure came from the Hetzner console report at server creation and was carried into the v98 brief as fact. It was a misreport. The box's real public IPv4 is 45.31.47.19 (eth0's actual DHCP lease, the source of all its working outbound IPv4; self-connect answers instantly, while 185.31.47.19 times out even from the server). The earlier "egress proxy drops IPv4 to this host" mystery was the same error — those connects were aimed at an address that is not this server. The v98 history above is left as written (it records what was believed at ship time); this section is the correction of record.
- THE VERIFIED SERVER: https://45.31.47.19 — Valhalla 3.9.1 behind Caddy with a production Let's Encrypt IP certificate (shortlived profile, auto-renewed); full smoke test PASSED Oct 16 (GET /status 200; POST /route Test B detour 4.886 km, no 14 Street NW; exactly one Access-Control-Allow-Origin pinned to the app origin; OPTIONS preflight 204; unlisted paths 404), and external reachability was verified from outside the box with full TLS chain validation. When the cPanel DNS zone is eventually reached, the A record is nav -> 45.31.47.19 (NOT 185.31.47.19).
- THE FIX: every occurrence of the dead address in shipped code replaced with https://45.31.47.19 — modules/navigator.js (NAV_DEFAULT_SERVER_URL) and modules/preferences.js (the module-load fallback literal; the navigator-test static parity pin holds — both copies equal). No other behaviour changed: the default still applies ONLY when no serverUrl key was ever saved; a saved custom address wins; a deliberately cleared '' stays cleared.
- Tests: navigator-test address pins moved to the corrected address — 84/84 green (Route POSTs exactly https://45.31.47.19/route in the never-saved world). Standing webVersion pins moved per convention: background-day E8 98→99 (39/39 green), chatbot H2 98→99. FULL SUITE: baseline captured before the round (33 suites green; 8 with standing failures); after — per-suite pass counts identical, the same 8 suites fail on the same checks (standing per-file-pin mismatches on unchanged files, names identical modulo the webVersion number they embed; manifest-export f1 name-identical, only its embedded wall-clock differs, as documented since v79).
