- **v126 — LIVE-MILES REBUILD (Oct 17, 2026, shipped; local 1801abd → remote 74349fb via gh-push-api.py; live webVersion 126, 43/43 manifest hashes verified against live bytes): the 4th straight day of the tracker under-counting (owner evidence Oct 17: dashboard 09:05 "Miles (live): 13.3 km" while his odometer proves 45 real km by 09:27 — 169,166 → 169,211 — with the entire ~31 km home→site leg absent; Oct 14–16 the same pattern).** THE SEAM, proven against the shipped engine before any fix: the v63 native-crumb merge deduped by the live ANCHOR'S TIMESTAMP — every crumb at or before the anchor counted as "already observed". But the first live fix after a relaunch takes the join path (begin() sets joinZero; ingest re-anchors WITHOUT counting the join segment), so the anchor TELEPORTS past ground the web stream never counted — and on the owner's real day the wake order is always fix-first (app.js boot starts LiveMiles at line ~2978, the ShiftTrack drain lands after; the 45 s drain loop and the visibilitychange drain race the same way mid-day), so the native service's run over that ground was skipped wholesale, then ACKED — deleted natively — by the drain. Gone for good. Reproduction on the v125 engine with the owner's exact pattern: 0.50 km counted of a 24.27 km route. THE FIX (engine only, js/miles.js; no UI, no settings, no money paths touched): (1) **counted-coverage intervals** — every segment folded into the total registers its time span in `segs` (merged, sorted, persisted in the snapshot); merges dedupe against `segs`, NEVER against the anchor. (2) **join origins survive** — a join re-anchor preserves the point it teleported FROM as `joinFrom` (earliest unconsumed origin wins across repeated relaunches); a merge seeds its chain from `joinFrom` when one is outstanding, else from the live anchor. (3) **chain-walk merge** — the crumb run is walked once in time order: covered spans advance the chain and count nothing (exactly once, idempotent re-feeds verified); the seed (join) segment counts ONLY when the first crumb lands within OBS_GAP_MAX_MS of the seed — the same honesty gate as v63 — otherwise gapJoins reports it, the first crumb becomes the chain start, and the unobserved stretch stays uncounted (never bridged, never straight-lined silently); later segments take the SAME filters as live fixes (accuracy > 50 m dropped, sub-10 m jitter accumulates against the chain point, teleport points dropped); the walk's end becomes the anchor when it is newer, so the next live fix continues the same chain. (4) **migration** — pre-v126 records carry no `segs`: begin() seeds coverage from the record's own crumb span (the trail its total was counted over) so a post-upgrade drain cannot double-count pre-upgrade ground; that span can over-claim at most the one join segment the old path once skipped inside it, and only until the day's record rolls over. PROOF (new tools/miles-rebuild-test.js, 10/10): a realistic 24.27 km Cornerstone NE → Deerfoot → Stoney → Maple Ridge polyline (the owner's actual Oct 17 dispatch run) driven in his pattern — live fixes for the first 500 m, app dead, service crumbs every ~600 m, wake AT the pit: **(a) wake fix first, then crumbs: 24.15 km counted (pre-fix: 0.50 km)**; (b) crumbs first, then fix: the same 24.15 km (order-proof); (c) two kill/wake cycles with a teleporting wake fix at each stop and one late fold: 23.85 km, exactly once (the small residue is whole-segment conservatism where a crumb span partially overlaps an already-counted span — the walk counts a span all-or-nothing rather than interpolate); re-feeding the run is a no-op; **(d) honesty: service ALSO dead for the first ~14 km → only the observed ~10.8 km counts, the join is reported as a gap join, the missing stretch is NOT invented.** native-track 35/35 UNCHANGED (its clean-order merge scenarios, the true-gap pin, the ack gate all stand), wake-hold 114/114 (the real-glue consumer). FULL SUITE (57 files): fail set EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows (background-day clock · chatbot H2 stale-104, now reading "got 126" · manifest-export f1 seconds fixture) — verified identical on the HEAD tree. Index pins: the 20 webVersion-cohort pins 125 → 126 (the ?v=96 and ?v=102 per-file cohorts untouched, per standing discipline); manifest regenerated — hash changes vs v125 are index.html + js/miles.js ONLY (local manifest carries 44 entries; the CI/live manifest carries 43 — it excludes eng.traineddata, the standing convention behind every "43/43" in this log). SHIP CATCH, recorded plainly: gh-push-api.py pushes ONLY the HEAD~1..HEAD diff — the first push (remote 3fe2cea) carried just the bump commit (index + version), so the first live deploy said webVersion 126 while still serving the OLD miles.js; live-byte verification caught it (live miles.js hash = the v125 file), the two local commits were squashed into 1801abd and re-pushed (remote 74349fb), and the re-deploy verified clean — the lesson stands: a version.json that says the new number is NOT proof the new code is live; only the per-file live hashes are. RESIDUAL GAPS, honestly: (i) if the phone OS kills BOTH the WebView and the native foreground service for a stretch (battery not Unrestricted, OEM killer), nobody observed that stretch and nothing in this build invents it — it surfaces as a gap in the day's reconciliation against the odometer, per the standing honesty rules; the once-per-session battery banner (v92) remains the mitigation. (ii) The web layer still creates no session while never opened (v123's noted limit) — the native arm's record for such a day folds via foldIntoRecord on the next open, now through the same coverage-correct engine. (iii) Spans are counted all-or-nothing at crumb granularity: a partially-covered span's uncovered tail is left uncounted (bounded by the service's crumb spacing, ~1–2% worst case in the 600 m-spaced proof rig; the live service cadence is denser). Per the USER-PROOF standard this is machine-verified preparation — the verdict is the owner's next real day: his dashboard miles against his odometer tonight.
- **v127 — MAINTENANCE / FUEL CAPTURE (Oct 17, 2026, shipped; ONE local commit — code + tests + this entry — pushed via gh-push-api.py as a single HEAD~1..HEAD diff, the v126 lesson applied; live webVersion 127, per-file live hashes verified against the live version.json, not just the number):** eight items, every one from the owner's Oct 17 field test, in his words. **(a) THE 2-SECOND SUGGESTION (09:32 — "the suggestion showed ~2 SECONDS then disappeared before he could tap it").** Mechanism, code-proven: the Power Read's tap-to-use chip lived in the SHARED `#<prefix>_chips` box, and the background candidate hunt's `onUpdate` blanks that box wholesale (`box.innerHTML = ''`) on EVERY pass before re-rendering only its own candidates; the pooled hunt runs seconds behind the ~2 s cloud answer, so its next pass erased a suggestion it never painted. THE FIX (js/app.js): the suggestion gets its OWN box (`#<prefix>_prbox`, `prBox`/`prSuggest`) that no other producer writes; it is cleared only by the user's own acts — tapping the value (accept), tapping "✕ Not it" (dismiss), or snapping a NEW photo (handleFile) — and an empty later hunt pass no longer blanks the hunt box either. Both power-read branches (odometer + safety sticker) route through it, so Fuel's shared pipeline inherits the rule. **(b) STICKER PHOTO IN MAINTENANCE (09:32):** a "🛡️ Annual safety inspection" card with the same camera/file pair, wired fill-only through the shared pipeline (`_wirePhotoOCR('mt_safety','safety', no-op, {origin: maintenance})` — steppers + power escalation come free); its Save is the confirm and writes BOTH stores (mt_annualDate + the onboarding.safetyExpiry mirror, the v118 rule), so no screen can resurrect a stale date. **(c) TREND HONESTY (09:14 — "Fuel trend 2.5 km/L · Last 1 fill-ups" off ONE leg):** under 3 legs the card says "Last leg — a trend needs 3 legs; you have N so far" (legs, not fill-ups; no trend claim); ≥3 legs reads "Last N legs". The v109 leg sanity filter (0 < km ≤ 50,000, 0.5–20 km/L) is untouched. **(d) ECONOMY UNIT (09:33 — "there has to be a way to change the unit… some users prefer mpg, not km/L"):** Preferences → "Fuel economy shown as" (km/L · L/100 km · MPG US), set-once, persists on change to `mt_econUnit`, default km/L; every economy print (Fuel trend, Maintenance summary + lifetime) converts at print time via `App._econConvert/_econFormat` (L/100 km = 100/v; MPG = v × 2.3521458 US) — records stay km/L. **(e) LIFETIME TOTALS (09:39):** the Maintenance summary shows Lifetime distance / Lifetime fuel / Lifetime economy with the honest span label ("from your fill-up records, FIRST → LAST (N fill-ups)… distance and economy count the legs between fill-ups with odometer readings; fuel counts every fill-up") — fuel sums EVERY fill (the poisoned-stamp fill still burned diesel: 432.8 L across his 5), distance/economy count only sane legs (656 km, 2.52 km/L on his October fixtures). **(f) THE RECEIPT PHOTO IS KEPT (09:53 — "Why is there no photo entry?... The picture I took yesterday?"):** code-proven before the fix — the Fuel save built `{date, time, odo, litres, cost, station}` and never wrote a photo field, discarding the captured image after OCR while the v118 details page read `rec.photo`. Save now attaches the already-downscaled 1600px OCR rendition; edit replaces it with a fresh photo or keeps the old; if the photo can't fit storage, the save retries ONCE without it (edit falls back to the previous photo) — the entry always saves and the drop is said once in the save note. **(g) MANIFEST INCOME LABEL (09:56):** while the day's shift is open, the earned row reads "Income earned today so far"; once closed, "Total Earned Income" — both renderers (preview + PDF lines); the settings toggle name stays. **(h) ODOMETER HISTORY (09:57 — "You should fix that"):** (1) `seedOdoReadings()` runs at app open and derives dated-log entries from records the app already holds — fills as an ascending chain (backwards stamps, >50,000 km leaps, and stamps > master + 1,000 skipped, so the Oct 15 invoice-number stamp 519,326 can NEVER seed), sessions at their start/end stamps — tagged 'seed-fill'/'seed-session', deduped on value|date so re-running adds nothing; the manifest's v122 boundary rules are untouched, the seed only supplies evidence. (2) "＋ Add a past reading" in Maintenance's odometer card: date/time/value → `App._logOdoReading(v, 'past', ts, isCorrection)` — lands with its true timestamp, never touches the master, box stays open for several entries, duplicates are said ("already recorded"). The owner's approved backfill (Oct 15 start 168,759 · Oct 15 end/Oct 16 start 168,936 · Oct 16 end 169,166 @ Oct 16 19:26) goes through this door in the app — deliberately NOT hard-coded into the build. PROOF: NEW tools/capture-v127-test.js **140/140** — the suggestion survives delayed hunt passes (empty AND non-empty), dismiss clears only its box, accept fills+persists, a new photo clears; sticker statics + wiring + Save dual-store write; trend copy at 1/2/3 legs; conversion exactness (round-trips km/L ↔ L/100 km ↔ MPG) + default/invalid fallback + render-level unit switch in both modules; lifetime sums from his real October fixtures incl. the poisoned stamp; photo save→details round-trip, no-photo state, quota drop-and-say-so, edit replace/keep/fallback; manifest label live (paused + running) vs closed in preview AND pdf; seed counts/sources/idempotence/poison exclusion; past readings land with chosen ts and bound Oct 15 on the manifest (168,759 → 168,936). Deliberate pin moves, cited in-suite: power-read-safety (the removed `b.textContent` line → the prSuggest call), maint-odo-photo + power-read-integration (chips now counted in #*_prbox, dismiss excluded). FULL SUITE (58 suites + OCR harness 32/32): fail set = EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows (background-day stale-version assert, chatbot H2 stale-104 assert — now reading "got 127", same row — manifest-export f1 seconds fixture). webVersion 126 → 127; the 20 webVersion-cohort index pins moved to ?v=127 (?v=96 / ?v=102 cohorts untouched); OTA manifest regenerated (44 local entries; live carries 43 — eng.traineddata excluded, the standing convention). RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly: (i) the queued-result path is a different surface — a power read that finishes while the user is elsewhere is still offered via the peek lines (Maintenance status line, Fuel prefill), which do not auto-clear but also do not use the prbox; the 2-second eraser existed only in the live pipeline and is gone there. (ii) Hunt chips from an earlier pass of the SAME photo now linger on an empty later pass by design — they are that photo's candidates, cleared by a tap or a new photo. (iii) The kept receipt photo is the 1600px OCR rendition, not the camera's original — that is the rendition the pipeline holds; it is what the details page shows. (iv) The seed can only derive from fills/sessions that exist on the device; a day evidenced ONLY by the undated master cannot be seeded (the master proves a value, not a moment — inventing the moment would break the honesty rule), which is exactly the gap the past-reading door covers. (v) Lifetime figures cover the records' span only and say so on the card; pre-app paper history enters through the same door, not by computation. Per the USER-PROOF standard all of this is machine-verified preparation — the verdict is the owner's hands: tonight's backfill through the new door, tomorrow's dash photo, and whether the suggestion is still standing when he reaches for it.
- **v128 — NAVIGATOR: THE TICK MUTATES, THE DETAIL SITS AT THE TAP (Oct 17, 2026, shipped; ONE local commit — code + tests + this entry — pushed via gh-push-api.py as a single HEAD~1..HEAD diff, the v126 lesson applied; live webVersion 128, per-file live hashes verified against live bytes, not just the number):** two owner recordings from the morning's drive, Navigator only. **ITEM 1 — THE DRIVE OVERLAY BLINK (10:25 recording, frame-analyzed by the parent: 26 frames @8fps diffed — map static ~0.75 s, then two consecutive whole-map changes repeating ~1×/sec; one frame caught the route line GONE, the puck's dot GONE, the arrival text MISSING from the bar, the banner clipped mid-render, tiles unmoved throughout).** v125's tile flicker stayed fixed; this was the layer ABOVE the tiles. THE MECHANISM, code-proven in modules/navigator.js: `driveTick()` — fired on every GPS fix (~1 Hz) — called `S.driveMap.setView(S.pos, 17)` unconditionally. In Leaflet 1.9.4 a same-zoom setView takes the ANIMATED pan path when the center moved, and the previous handler was still mid-flight at the next fix, so the pan restarted per fix; every pan 'move' re-projects the SVG overlay paths (route line + position puck) — inside a plane CSS-3D-transformed at 340% size under rotateX(52°), so each re-projection is a full re-raster of a giant layer, and the compositor drops torn frames exactly as recorded. Compounding it: the heading spin wrote `style.transform` on ANY heading change (GPS heading jitters degree-by-degree at speed), each write restarting the .45s transition on the same giant plane — near-constant re-rasterization. And the reroute pill rewrote its class + text every tick. THE FIX (driveTick, mutation only): the recenter is a DEADBAND panTo — `S.driveMap.getCenter()` read per tick, `panTo(S.pos, { animate: false })` only when the fix is >3 m from the current center (Leaflet documents animate:false as an instant, animation-free pan: the existing layers move once, in place; standstill jitter recenters nothing at all); the spin write is gated by a ≥2° deadband (new pure helper `angDeltaDeg`; a real turn clears 2° in one fix, jitter never reaches it); the reroute pill's class + text are written only when its shown state flips (the `rrShown` state cache, reset per route in startDrive/driveOnRoute). The marker (setLatLng) and the retracting line (setLatLngs, v125) were already mutations and are unchanged; a re-route still rebuilds the line ONCE per route (driveOnRoute, an event, not a tick). Everything v124/v125 won is untouched: Esri basemap via driveTileSpec, deep-blue #1a56db 12px core + 17px white casing retracting as travelled, ETA stack, full-bleed map, floating chrome, landscape compact banner, chat FAB hidden while driving, 52° tilt, fadeAnimation:false + keepBuffer:4. PROOF: navigator-test's fake Leaflet is now per-map instrumented (layer adds/removes, setView calls, panTos per map; layers count their own setLatLng/setLatLngs; elements count innerHTML assignments) — six real driveTicks along the Test B line assert ZERO layer adds, ZERO layer removes, ZERO setView calls and ZERO banner/bar innerHTML assignments on the drive map, ≥5 instant panTos (the ticks DID recenter), setLatLngs fired on both line layers (retraction by mutation), the arrival clock still updating by text, and the spin deadband behaving (1° jitter: no write; 6° turn: writes). navigator-test 179/179; navigator-screen gains the static (e) pins 40/40. **ITEM 2 — TAP-A-PLACE ACTIONS AT THE TAP (10:27 recording: he tapped "Ivor East Pond" and its detail — coords, "2 visits · last today 10:25 AM", Navigate, Remove — rendered BELOW the entire recents list; his words: "When clicked on the site! Navigate should pop up right there, user shouldn't have to scroll down the page to find it!").** THE FIX (modules/navigation.js, the merged Navigator's places face): the ONE detail panel now MOVES in the DOM — `placeDetail()` inserts the existing `#nv_detail` node immediately after the tapped row inside `#nv_results` (accordion-style, in place; Navigate, the one primary, is the first action inside it, on screen at the tap). Tapping another place moves the same single node under that row (the previous expansion collapses by construction — there is only ever one panel). Every list repaint first calls `detailToHome()` so the panel is never stranded or destroyed inside a list being replaced, and re-places under the still-selected row after the paint; a selection with no row on screen (a Recent-destinations pick, a filtered-out selection, an empty result) leaves the panel at its home slot under the list, exactly as before. The v113 tap rule is untouched — the tap still writes the place into the search field and moves the ✓ in place with no list repaint (pinned by identity: the row elements after a tap are the same objects). New CSS: inside the list the panel reads as an expansion (indent + accent edge). SAME ITEM, the subtitle: `pickerRowInner` appended the place's ADDRESS raw, and a favorite whose address IS its pin (the owner's Butterfly: address "50.9519460, -114.0273550") printed raw coordinates in the row — now gated by the existing v118 `nameIsCoords` test (a real street address still shows; the Recent-destinations card's address line got the same gate). Coordinates remain visible in exactly one place: inside the expanded detail. PROOF: NEW tools/navigator-v128-test.js **22/22** — drives the REAL render on a small ordered DOM (real parent/child/sibling structure + an innerHTML parser, because the defect IS DOM order and the older stubs keep none): recents paint in order; no row contains the coordinate pair while a real address still shows; a tap puts the detail IMMEDIATELY after the tapped row (adjacency, with a row still following it — not the list's bottom), exactly one panel tree-wide, Navigate wired inside it, the coordinates present in the detail; tapping two more places collapses/moves the expansion each time; filter repaints (selected place filtered out, zero matches) leave the panel whole at home, and tapping a row from a repainted list expands under that row. nav-dropdown 64/64 and nav-places 60/60 unchanged-green (their stubs lack insertBefore/parentNode — placeDetail/detailToHome no-op safely there, by construction). FULL SUITE (59 suites + OCR harness 32/32): fail set = EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows (background-day E8 stale-version assert, chatbot H2 stale-104 assert — now reading "got 128", same row — manifest-export f1 seconds fixture). webVersion 127 → 128; the 20 webVersion-cohort index pins moved to ?v=128 (?v=96 / ?v=102 cohorts untouched); OTA manifest regenerated (44 local entries; live carries 43 — eng.traineddata excluded, the standing convention). Files changed: modules/navigator.js, modules/navigation.js, css/style.css, index.html, version.json, tools/navigator-test.js, tools/navigator-screen-test.js, tools/navigator-v128-test.js (new), BUGS.md. RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly: (i) the blink's proof is the instrumented real code path in the harness (zero rebuild operations per tick), not a frame-diff of the owner's phone — the compositor behavior that turned per-tick re-projection into dropped frames is browser-internal; the on-glass verdict is his next drive, per the USER-PROOF standard. (ii) The planning map's follow painting (paintPos) still recenters per fix while Drive is open — it is the hidden map underneath, the v104 single-writer design, and it drops nothing on the drive map's overlay pane; left unchanged deliberately. (iii) During a re-route fetch the pill now flips at most twice (off-route → rerouting → hidden) instead of rewriting per tick — intended. (iv) "Reachable without scrolling" is pinned as DOM adjacency to the just-tapped (on-screen) row — the strongest statement a DOM suite can make without a layout engine; if a driver runs a very short viewport in landscape with the sheet scrolled deep, the expansion opens at the tap point itself, which is the position his thumb already proved visible.
- **v129 — DISPATCH: A CLOSED THING LOOKS CLOSED (Oct 17, 2026, shipped; ONE local commit — code + tests + this entry — pushed via gh-push-api.py as a single HEAD~1..HEAD diff, the v126 lesson applied; live webVersion 129, per-file live hashes verified against live bytes, not just the number): the owner's Oct 16 Aecon dispatch (ticket #22729), from his 10:28 screenshot and his 10:34 words: "Can you close that dispatch with that picture? I sent you yesterday?"** Opening the COMPLETED dispatch dropped him into the Edit form with Material blank, Ticket # blank, Amount 0.00 — because the day's close had written the hours (foreman 10.0 + 1 travel = 11.0 h) and the pay ($319.00) into Revenue and the manifest, but never onto the dispatch record or its ticket objects; the ticket lived on the record as the haul-card PHOTO and its number was never parsed into the record, so dspEditFieldsFor honestly showed blanks and the owner read a closed day as un-closed — with a live "Save changes" button that could have written the blanks back over the record. Dispatch module only; completion money math, Revenue writing, session behaviour and the gibberish gate untouched. **(1) THE COMPLETED FACE.** A completed history row now offers "👁 View" (primary) instead of Edit; View opens a read-complete summary card at the top of the module (with the v75-style come-to-the-user scroll): Status "Completed · <when>", job site, date, truck, material, ticket #, Foreman hours / Travel / Total hours from the ticket summary (net weight on weight days), loads, the amount earned for the day, the ticket photo(s), the notes — and only BELOW it, "✏️ Edit this record" (secondary) + Done. The amount is priced by the SAME shared per-day builder the manifest and invoice print from (Meticulous.shared.buildRevenueLines over the day's rev_entries + rev_extras at the account rate/standard — new dspDayEarned mirrors manifest collect()'s assembly, including its legacy fallback; loads come from the shared dayLoads over the day's records, new dspDayLoadsFor). Edit therefore is never a completed record's opening face. **(2) THE CLOSE WRITES THE RECORD.** Both close paths — the ticket-photo Confirm and plain Complete — now build the closed record through dspBuildClosed (the manual path previously mutated the active object in place), assemble the ONE completion plan via dspCompletionFor, stamp the close facts onto the record BEFORE the write (new dspApplyCloseFacts: ticket # from the plan's ticket numbers or mined from the tickets, material mined from the tickets, the day amount = the plan entry's amount — the exact dollars Revenue receives), and hand that same plan to applyCompletion (which now accepts a pre-assembled plan — assembled exactly once per close; the close-preview/Confirm identity is preserved). The facts also land on each ticket that lacks its own. Records closed BEFORE this build heal at READ time only (new dspTicketFacts mines parsed ticket fields first, then the tickets' stored docRead reports through the v117 gibberish gate — a dirty entry is never a fact; dspCloseFactsFor prefers stored fields, dspCompletedFace assembles the healed view) — reading never rewrites stored data, pinned byte-for-byte. dspEditFieldsFor now pre-fills from the same miner, so the owner's Oct 16 form opens with 22729 / Clay instead of blanks. **(3) EDIT SAFETY.** dspApplyEdit gains the completed-record guard: when the original is completed, a form field left blank for ticket # / material / amount keeps the close's value (healed values included) — a save can no longer stomp a closed day's truth with blanks — while a value the form DID carry still corrects the fact, and every other field edits freely; open records are unguarded, exactly as before. The edit form SAYS so when the record is completed ("…clearing one will not erase what the close recorded"). A side effect, stated plainly: because the record now carries the amount before applyCompletion reads it, the completion announcement is the amount-carrying one the module has always used for amount-holding records ("Dispatch completed — $319.00 logged to Revenue.") — it names the exact dollars logged, like the manifest feed line beside it. PROOF: NEW tools/dispatch-completed-test.js **64/64** — region world (verbatim DSP regions + the REAL store + the REAL ticket-ocr + the REAL INV-CYCLE builder): miner precedence + gibberish rejection, dspCloseFactsFor/dspEditFieldsFor healing on the owner's exact Oct 16 shape, dspApplyCloseFacts (fill-only, plan amount, ticket self-stamping, no input mutation), dspCompletedFace (hours 10 + 1 = 11; the builder's $319.00 with a same-day tracker entry NOT stacked — the $601.75 lesson; the stored record byte-identical after the read), the dspApplyEdit guard matrix; module world (the whole module driven like a finger): View-not-Edit rows, the face's contents and its Edit-below placement, Done, both close paths' stamps asserted on the stored record AND the ticket, and the full stomp drive (face → Edit this record → clear all three close-fact fields → Save changes → facts intact, the real edit saved). Deliberate pin moves, cited in-suite: dispatch-close (a) (the completion announcement now names the stamped amount — see above) and dispatch-intake (the dspCleanDocRead call-site count 2 → 3: the facts miner cleans through the same gate before mining; the two render sites unchanged, the pin stays exact). FULL SUITE (60 suites + OCR harness 32/32): fail set = EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows (background-day E8 stale-version assert, chatbot H2 stale-104 assert — now reading "got 129", same row — manifest-export f1 seconds fixture), verified against the pristine-tree baseline before the build. webVersion 128 → 129; the 20 webVersion-cohort index pins moved to ?v=129 (?v=96 / ?v=102 cohorts untouched); OTA manifest regenerated (44 local entries; live carries 43 — eng.traineddata excluded, the standing convention); manifest hash changes vs v128 are index.html + modules/dispatch.js ONLY. RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly: (i) read-time healing derives ticket # / material only from what the record already holds (parsed ticket fields, stored read reports) — a ticket whose read found neither (a photo-only close with a failed read) still opens with those two facts honestly absent; its hours/amount/photo/status all show. (ii) The face's amount is the DAY's builder total (the manifest's number for that date), so on a multi-dispatch day it is the day's earned, not a per-dispatch split — the same truth the manifest prints, labelled "Amount earned". (iii) The close-fact guard keys on the record being completed at edit time; a record completed outside the two UI close paths (none exist in the app today — verified by grep: dspBuildClosed + the Complete handler are the only completion writes) would carry unstamped fields and rely on the same read-healing as pre-v129 records. (iv) The face is a module-level view state (viewCompletedId), closed by Done / Back / entering Edit — it does not survive an app restart, by design: it is a view, not data. Per the USER-PROOF standard this is machine-verified preparation — the verdict is the owner's hands: his Oct 16 dispatch should now open on its closed face, ticket #22729 and $319.00 in place.
