- **v131 — CONNECT: FINDING AND CONNECTING PEOPLE GOES LIVE (Oct 17, 2026, shipped; ONE local commit — code + tests + rules + this entry — pushed via gh-push-api.py as a single HEAD~1..HEAD diff, the v126 lesson applied; live webVersion 131, per-file live hashes verified against live bytes, not just the number): the owner's order, Oct 17 11:11 MDT — "Then fix it before he tries."** This morning he had told Umar (a first-cohort driver) to set the app up and "search me as a driver in Connect" — and the shipped Connect was Phase-1 local-only, so that search could not work: the only "invite" door (P&L, v120) saved a pending driver on the sender's own phone and delivered nothing. This build is the HANDSHAKE slice of the Phase-2 blueprint (connect-phase2-blueprint.md): find, request, accept — live. Dispatch sharing, shared records and the two-books pipe are NOT in this build and are nowhere claimed. **(1) THE DIRECTORY (new js/connectlive.js, the account-to-account engine).** Every signed-in account publishes `directory/{uid}` — {name, business, role} plus derived lowercase/prefix/token indexes and a timestamp, NOTHING else: no email, no rates, no contracting company (the company a driver drives FOR is never published — proven negative in tests). The display name is the account's trading name (onboarding.companyName — the name the account invoices under); a brand-new account that hasn't set one yet publishes an email-derived name (split, title-cased — the email itself is never stored) and heals to the real name the moment Preferences saves it. Written at signup (the onboarding finish), and SELF-HEALED on every signed-in app start through the App.onAuth funnel (which Preferences save also calls), with a content guard so an unchanged identity writes nothing — so the two EXISTING accounts (the owner + Ahmed) become findable the next time each opens the app, no re-signup, no manual step. **(2) SEARCH.** The Connect screen gains a Find people card: one input + one primary Search button; the engine unions a token lookup (any whole name/business word), name-prefix and business-prefix ranges over the directory, then requires every typed word to appear — "Asjad", "kamal", "Asj", "asjad kamal" all find the owner; results show name + business + role; you are never your own result. Umar's exact path: P&L's "🤝 Connect a driver" (v120) now ROUTES into this same live search (a flag lands him on the search box); the v120 email/phone invite form and its local-only save are REMOVED (the button remains the one primary; "Add manually instead" is untouched, and what YOU pay a driver is still set on your own books and never crosses). **(3) REQUEST + ACCEPT.** Tapping Connect on a result writes `requests/{from}_{to}` — the pair, identity snapshots for both sides, status pending, timestamps; one doc per pair, ever. The other account's Connect screen carries a Requests card (incoming with Accept / Decline; outgoing with its honest state; a declined request can be re-sent from either the results row or the Requests card). On Accept, BOTH accounts gain the connection in their own cloud data in the EXISTING con_list shape extended with the peer uid + status (viaConnect): the accepter's side lands immediately in the Accept handler; the requester's side lands via reconcile, which runs on every app start and Connect open (deferred until CloudSync's first pull settles, so a pre-pull local copy can't stomp the cloud one) and is idempotent. Pending reads "Request sent — waiting" on the sender and "wants to connect" on the recipient; declined reads "Request declined" on BOTH sides; the module status line counts waiting requests. Deleting a linked connection records the peer uid in con_hidden so reconcile never resurrects it on that device. Manual (typed) connections and every Phase-1 consumer — share pickers, History, previews — behave exactly as before; share buttons render only on unlinked or CONNECTED rows, never on a pending/declined link. **(4) RULES.** firestore.rules: the users tree stays owner-only; `directory/{uid}` is readable by any signed-in user and writable only by its owner (writes must carry the identity strings); `requests/{id}` is readable by the two parties only, creatable only by the sender and only as pending, updatable only as (recipient: pending → accepted/declined) or (sender: declined → pending re-send) with the pair immutable, and never deletable. Deployed through the project's existing pipeline — .github/workflows/deploy.yml runs `firebase deploy --only hosting,firestore:rules` on push to main, so the rules ride the same push as the code. **(5) HONEST COPY.** The Phase-1 handshake disclaimers are gone ("no invite leaves the phone", "arrives in a later update", "Invites go live with Connect"); what is not built says so plainly where the UI needs the space: "Dispatch & document sharing arrives next" — finding and connecting are described as live because they are. PROOF: NEW tools/connect-live-test.js **83/83** — the real engine driven over a fake compat Firestore by THREE user worlds (owner / a fleet manager / a driver with no company name yet) sharing one database: publish field-set exactness + the no-email/no-rates/no-contracting-company negatives, no-op republish vs heal-on-change, the email-fallback name, the search matrix incl. self-exclusion and the never-published contracting company finding NOBODY, request → seen → accept → BOTH con_lists carrying the uid link, duplicate-send and send-after-accept, decline → honest both sides → re-send re-pends, pending-incoming creates no entry, con_hidden blocks resurrection, manual entries byte-untouched, the onAuth publish funnel, signed-out/signed-in render smoke, and the rules-file access shapes asserted as text. Deliberate pin moves, cited in-suite: pnl-flawless (the v120 verbatim note → the live wording; the "invite lands a con_list connection" pin → the route-to-live-search pin; the pending status line follows the note), boss-firstrun (the v76 honesty-line string → the sharing-scoped line; the v76 TWO-card count → FOUR, the handshake's Find people + Requests cards). card-previews passes unchanged (ConnectLive absent there → legacy status strings byte-identical). FULL SUITE (61 suites + OCR harness 32/32): fail set = EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows (background-day stale-version assert, chatbot H2 stale-104 assert — now reading "got 131", same row — manifest-export f1 seconds fixture), verified against a pristine-v130-tree baseline taken before the build. webVersion 130 → 131; the 20 webVersion-cohort index pins moved to ?v=131 (?v=96 / ?v=102 cohorts untouched); OTA manifest regenerated (45 local entries — the engine is a new file; live carries 44 by the same count convention that made v130's 44 local / 43 live — eng.traineddata excluded, the standing convention). Files changed: js/connectlive.js (new), modules/connect.js, modules/pnl.js, js/app.js, firestore.rules, index.html, version.json, tools/connect-live-test.js (new), tools/pnl-flawless-test.js, tools/boss-firstrun-test.js, BUGS.md. RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly: (i) the directory is readable by ANY signed-in account by design — it carries identity only (name/business/role); there is no browse-all view in the UI, search is the only door, and at today's two-account scale that is the intended trade — revisit before any public launch. (ii) Search recall depends on the published name: a driver who never set a company/trading name is findable only by the email-derived name until they fill Preferences (the record self-heals on the next open after they do). (iii) Removing a linked connection is local-only: the peer keeps their entry and the accepted request stands — there is no disconnect/un-accept flow in this slice; con_hidden stops THIS device resurrecting it. (iv) Request docs carry identity snapshots from send time; if a peer renames afterwards, the request row shows the name as sent until the connection materializes (the con_list entry then carries the request-time name until a fresh reconcile writes the current one — reconcile refreshes name/role on every run, so it converges on the next app start). (v) The existing accounts publish on their NEXT app open after this build — there is no server-side backfill (none exists in this architecture), so Umar finds the owner once the owner's app has opened on v131 at least once; both men open the app daily, and the owner was told to restart for this build anyway. (vi) Dispatch sharing between connected pairs is NOT built — a connected pair sees the connection and the "arrives next" note, nothing more. Per the USER-PROOF standard this is machine-verified preparation — the verdict is the owner's hands: Umar searches "Asjad", taps Connect, the owner accepts in Connect, and both books show the link.
- **v132 — CONNECT: THE PIPE — HIS DAY AGGREGATES ON THE BOSS'S DASHBOARD BY ITSELF (Oct 17, 2026, shipped; ONE local commit — code + tests + rules + this entry — pushed via gh-push-api.py as a single HEAD~1..HEAD diff, the v126 lesson applied; live webVersion 132, per-file live hashes verified against live bytes, not just the number): the owner's mandate, Oct 17 11:23 MDT — "So all my info has to self aggregate in his dashboard!" — with his 11:29 refinements: a haul ticket does DOUBLE DUTY on the boss's books (revenue at the client rate HE chooses; wages at (foreman + travel) x the pay rate HE sets for that driver — the standing haul-card model, his Oct 16: 10.0 + 1 = 11.0 h x $29 = $319.00), and the role-face discipline (his own owner-operator surface is unique and NOT a template; a pure driver's face stays cleaner than the owner-operator's; the boss face is the aggregation side).** This is the pipe slice of the Phase-2 blueprint (connect-phase2-blueprint.md) on top of the v131 handshake. DEVIATION from the blueprint, noted per the brief: the blueprint imagined a shared drawer inside the users tree; the shipped shape follows v131 instead — two TOP-LEVEL collections (shareddays, pipedispatches) beside directory/requests — because the users tree is owner-only by rule and stays that way. **(1) UP — THE SHARED DAY RECORD (new js/connectpipe.js).** `shareddays/{driverUid}_{date}`: ONE record per driver per day holding FACTS ONLY — the day's dispatches (driver-declared, WITH their source label per the blueprint, and boss-sent, each with its origin), every dispatch's tickets with their close facts (ticket #, material, loads, foreman hours, the fixed travel hour from the close's own ticket summary, net weight, a photo REFERENCE — the photo bytes never cross; they stay in the driver's own tree), session hours (saved sessions + the live tracker, the manifest's derivation), the day's loads. NO rates, NO amounts, NO money of any kind enter the record — proven by exact key sets + bait scans in the suite (the driver's own $999 bait client rate, his stamped $319.00 record amount and the ticket photo's bytes all asserted ABSENT). The viewers list on each doc is the driver's accepted connections at publish time; publishing is gated on having viewers, content-guarded per session (facts-compared, updatedAt excluded from the key), and triggered by the auth funnel (today + yesterday), by every dispatch close (a guarded hook at the end of applyCompletion — both close paths end there), by an inbox landing, and by a fresh accept (Connect's refreshLive republishes). **(2) THE BOSS'S BOARD — AGGREGATED + PRICED PER TICKET.** The Dispatch screen gains, for office + owner-operator faces ONLY, a "👥 Crew today" board: per-driver day cards (working now / day done / not started, hours on the clock, loads, tickets closed) with every CLOSED ticket priced BOTH ways on the boss's side by the pipe's pure pricer — REVENUE = billable hours x the boss's client rate (billable = foreman; travel joins ONLY when the ticket's travelBillable flag says the customer agreed — default NO, the Oct 14 travel rule) and WAGES = (foreman + travel) x the pay rate the boss set for that driver in P&L (his pnl_drivers entry carrying the driver's uid) — plus day totals + margin, and the Dispatch home-card status line leads with the crew for boss faces. P&L gains the same aggregation for the month ("🤝 Connected crew", priced identically) as a READ-ONLY books view: it posts nothing into the wage log, so pipe work can never double-count hand-logged work. Unpriced is honest, never invented: no pay entry -> wages show unpriced with a "Set his pay rate" door (Dispatch board AND P&L card) that lands in P&L's manual form prefilled with the driver's name and stamps his uid onto the saved entry — that stamp is what prices his days from then on; no client rate -> revenue unpriced with a Preferences pointer; an OPEN dispatch prices nothing on either leg (the close is the money event). **(3) DOWN — BOSS-SENT DISPATCHES.** `pipedispatches/{id}`: the boss composes in Dispatch ("📤 Send a dispatch", defaulting to tomorrow per the cadence law) or via a connected connection's existing picker in Connect (which now REALLY sends for connected connections and marks the History entry "Sent ✓"; documents and manual connections still prepare locally, unchanged). The delivery lands in the driver's app as a SCHEDULED dispatch record for its own day — never made active, never disturbing an active same-day dispatch (suite-proven byte-identical), flagged pipeConflict when another open dispatch holds that date, FOR THE DRIVER'S PICK, never auto-decided — and flows back UP in his next day record with origin 'boss', so the boss's board expects the ticket. Receipt marks ride the doc (the Oct 14 WhatsApp-tick decision): sent -> delivered (his app materialized it) -> seen (he opened Dispatch with it there). A sent dispatch is SEALED — the rules let only the recipient advance the marks, with the content fields pinned immutable. Landing is idempotent (pipe_landed), seen-marking is once-ever (pipe_seen). **(4) ROLE FACES (owner refinement).** The owner-operator's own surface is byte-unchanged in shape (he gains the board/send/crew views because he is 'both' — a boss face — not because his account became a template). A pure DRIVER gains NO surface at all: no board, no compose card, no P&L crew card (all three are role-gated in source and suite-pinned); his side of the pipe is invisible plumbing — his day publishes by itself, arrivals get ONE line at the top of his Dispatch screen, and the records are simply his dispatches. The office face is the aggregation side, per the blueprint's five tools. **(5) RULES.** firestore.rules: shareddays readable only by its driver + the peers named in viewers, writable only by the driver it describes (driver field immutable); pipedispatches readable by the two parties, creatable only by the sender as 'sent', updatable only by the recipient advancing sent -> delivered -> seen with content sealed; the users tree stays owner-only. Deployed through the project's existing pipeline (deploy.yml: firebase deploy --only hosting,firestore:rules) — the Actions run for this push concluded SUCCESS and the deployed rules were verified live (see the ship line in this entry's header record). **(6) HONEST COPY.** Invoice + manifest SHARING between accounts is NOT in this slice and is the only "arrives next" left standing (Connect's one quiet line, the P&L note); the v76 Dispatch office line no longer says sending "arrives with Connect Phase 2" — it points at the send card on the same screen. PROOF: NEW tools/connect-pipe-test.js **77/77** — the REAL connectlive + connectpipe engines over a fake compat Firestore driven by FOUR user worlds (boss / the owner as driver / a second working driver / a stranger + a connection-less driver): publish fact-exactness + the no-rates/no-money/no-photo-bytes negatives, no-op republish, the priced ticket both ways at the boss's rates (10 x $52 = $520 revenue; 11 x $29 = $319 wages; travel-billable variant 11 x $52 = $572), unpriced honesty (no pay entry, no client rate, open dispatch), the down-flow (scheduled landing, active-day byte-untouched, conflict flag, delivered -> seen, idempotent re-pull, flows back up with origin), stranger/lone-driver privacy, the verbatim-extracted DSP-PIPE board region rendering the priced board + send card, and the rules/copy/gate statics. connect-live stays **83/83**. Deliberate pin moves, cited in-suite: boss-firstrun (the v76 F3 line -> the v132 wording pointing at the send card; the Connect honesty line -> invoice & manifest scoped) and connect-live ((g) honesty line -> invoice & manifest scoped; (h) "all sharing framed as future" -> only invoice/manifest framed as future, dispatch never). FULL SUITE (62 suites + OCR harness 32/32): fail set = EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows (background-day stale-version assert — now reading "got 132", same row — chatbot H2 stale-104 assert, manifest-export f1 seconds fixture), verified against a pristine-v131-tree baseline taken before the build. webVersion 131 -> 132; the 21 webVersion-cohort index pins moved to ?v=132 (?v=96 / ?v=102 cohorts untouched) and js/connectpipe.js added at ?v=132; OTA manifest regenerated (46 local entries; live carries 45 by the standing eng.traineddata convention). Files changed: js/connectpipe.js (new), js/app.js, modules/dispatch.js, modules/connect.js, modules/pnl.js, firestore.rules, index.html, version.json, tools/connect-pipe-test.js (new), tools/boss-firstrun-test.js, tools/connect-live-test.js, BUGS.md. RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly: (i) the ticket photo is a REFERENCE across the pipe (photoRef) — the boss sees that a photo exists and its ticket's facts, not the image; cross-account photo viewing needs a storage design this slice does not pretend to have. (ii) Freshness is event-driven, not a live socket: a day republishes on sign-in, on a dispatch close, on an accept, and on an inbox landing — a tracker-only stop (no dispatch close) refreshes the boss's board on the driver's next app start or Connect open, not the same minute. (iii) The priced crew views (Dispatch today, P&L month) are read-only aggregations — nothing posts into P&L's wage log or Revenue from the pipe in this slice; the boss's formal books still take their entries the existing ways, and invoice/manifest sharing (drivers' period invoices up as payables, client invoices down from closed tickets) arrives next, as the copy says. (iv) travelBillable rides the shared record (default NO per the Oct 14 rule) and the revenue leg honours it, but no UI sets the flag per ticket yet — it arrives with the ticket-correction/agreement surface; until then travel is the boss's absorbed cost in every priced view, which is the default the owner legislated. (v) Viewers are snapshotted per day doc at publish time: removing a connection stops FUTURE days flowing to that peer (v131's local-removal semantics stand — there is still no disconnect/un-accept flow), and days already shared stay readable by the peer they were published to. (vi) The crew board covers TODAY (Dispatch) and the current MONTH (P&L) — there is no historical day browser yet. Per the USER-PROOF standard this is machine-verified preparation — the verdict is the owner's hands: Umar closes a ticket, and his day — hours, loads, the closed ticket — is simply THERE on the boss's Dispatch board, priced at the boss's rates, with no rate of either man's anywhere on the other side.

- **v133 — NAVIGATOR DRIVE FACE: THE BLANKING FIXED STRUCTURALLY (Oct 17, 2026, shipped; ONE local commit — code + tests + this entry — pushed via gh-push-api.py as a single HEAD~1..HEAD diff, the v126 lesson applied; live webVersion 133, per-file live hashes verified against live bytes, not just the number): the owner's evidence, Oct 17 11:39–11:40 MDT, a 39 s screen recording (Deerfoot → Ivor East Pond) — on the v128/v132 code the Drive banner and the ETA bar TEXT VANISH ENTIRELY for seconds at a time, the navy containers staying painted while their content empties, alternating with correct frames from 350 m down to 120 m out, the guidance data underneath alive the whole time (distance counting, arrival holding). His words: "It's even worst then flickering.... The whole screen is disappearing now!"** This is the third build at this face (v125 tiles, v128 mutate-only tick) and the lesson is now written where it belongs: v128's instrumented proof — zero layer adds/removes, zero setView, zero banner/bar innerHTML across real driveTicks — was all TRUE and all beside the point, because the meter nobody had run was textContent (navigator-test's stub held it as a plain property, so node replacements were uncountable). **CHARACTERIZATION FIRST (new tools/navigator-v133-test.js, the real module on an instrumented DOM that counts every textContent assignment, pre-fix code):** the old driveTick assigned textContent on ALL FIVE chrome text nodes on EVERY GPS fix — 20 standstill re-fires = **100 assignments, 100 of them byte-identical** to what was already painted; 60 approach fixes = **300 assignments, 183 same-value** (the arrival clock alone was node-replaced 60 times for 6 real changes; the instruction 60 times for a handful of maneuvers). Every assignment REPLACES the text node and invalidates that element's paint; the parent navy boxes (painted once, rarely invalidated) survive on the surface while their constantly-invalidated text is exactly what a GPU-starved Android WebView compositor drops — containers stay, content empties, seconds at a time. And one vector needed no compositor at all: when the NEXT maneuver's instruction string is empty (a real Valhalla shape — maneuverPlan defaults a missing instruction to ''), the old tick wrote '' over a good instruction: **13 literal blank writes** in those same 60 fixes, during which the banner instruction was empty IN THE DATA. The CSS fed the starvation: .nv-drive-tilt carried will-change:transform on a rotateX(52deg) that is set once at mount and NEVER changes — a permanent 340%-plane promoted layer held open for the whole drive — while the chrome itself had NO layer promotion, so its raster fate was shared with the plane's surface. The paint-level starvation itself cannot be executed in this build environment (no browser here — stated plainly, per the brief); the DOM churn that feeds it is measured exactly above, and the fix removes both the churn and the starvation structure. **THE FIX (modules/navigator.js + css/style.css only): (1) ONE CACHED WRITER for all of the drive chrome's text — driveText(el, key, value) assigns ONLY when the value changes (cache lives on the render's own state bag, which is rebuilt with the DOM, so cache and glass can never disagree); an unchanged node is never touched and its paint never invalidated. The four guidance nodes (distance, instruction, arrival clock, arrival sub-line) write through driveTextKeep: a momentarily empty computed value is simply NOT written — the last good text stands; there is no cleared-then-refilled state anywhere on any tick path, and when data is momentarily unavailable the last good text is what stays on screen. The road sub-line may still clear (an unnamed road is a fact, and the banner's distance + instruction never clear). (2) EVERY WRITER AUDITED: driveTick (the five nodes), driveArrived (now through the same writer — its distance-clearing is the arrival state's one deliberate paint), paintMute (the bar's mute label is chrome text too — through the writer), the static HTML placeholders (painted at open, replaced by the first tick, never re-written), and outside the module: js/app.js (trackerTick paints the dashboard only; visibilitychange does wake-lock/autostart/power-read work; openModule re-renders only on the user's own navigation), js/cloudsync.js (visibility flush only), js/ota.js — NO other writer of the drive chrome exists, and no path paints the containers before/without content. (3) COMPOSITOR: .nv-drive-top and .nv-drive-bar now carry their OWN promotion (transform:translateZ(0) + will-change:transform) — two small, stable layers whose raster is no longer shared with the plane's; .nv-drive-tilt LOSES its will-change claim (static transform — pure budget burn); the spin KEEPS its claim (its transform genuinely animates on turns, already ≥2°-deadbanded since v128). (4) THE OVERLAY (same family, 10:25 evidence — line + puck dropping from single frames): audited — it has been mutate-only since v128 (setLatLng / setLatLngs, deadbanded instant panTo, no per-tick teardown or rebuild anywhere in the face) and stays exactly so; its drops ride the same starved surface, and this build cuts both feeders (the 1 Hz text invalidation churn, the wasted plane layer). **THE STRUCTURAL GUARANTEE:** while driving, no code path can leave the banner or the bar empty or repaint-pending — text changes only by single, changed-value assignments into dedicated nodes that hold their last good value, on chrome that owns its composited layers. Post-fix characterization on the same stressed drive (standstill repeats + 60 approach fixes + the empty-instruction gap maneuver): **0 same-value assignments, 0 empty guidance assignments, guidance painted after every tick**; writes on change only (distance 56, instruction 4, arrival clock 6 across 60 fixes). **PROOF:** NEW tools/navigator-v133-test.js **14/14**; navigator-test **179/179** (v128 blink pins + arrival all pass unchanged), navigator-screen **40/40**, navigator-v128 **22/22**, nav-places 86/86, nav-dropdown 64/64, overflow-lock 48/48 — **NO pin moves anywhere**. FULL SUITE (63 suites + OCR harness 32/32): fail set = EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows (background-day stale-version assert — parity-verified against the pristine v132 tree, 38 passed / 1 failed on both — chatbot H2 stale-104 assert, manifest-export f1 seconds fixture). webVersion 132 → 133; the 22 webVersion-cohort index pins moved to ?v=133; OTA manifest regenerated (46 files). Files changed: modules/navigator.js, css/style.css, index.html, version.json, tools/navigator-v133-test.js (new), BUGS.md. **RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly:** (i) His WebView's compositor cannot be executed from this environment — the guarantee is structural (nothing left to invalidate, nothing sharing the plane's raster), and per the USER-PROOF standard the verdict is his next drive, exactly as the brief required this entry to state. (ii) The route line + puck live INSIDE the transformed plane (Leaflet SVG) and still re-project on each deadbanded pan — mutate-only, never rebuilt; if single-frame overlay drops persist on his next drive while the chrome now holds, the next lever is plane/layer budget (plane size vs. quality), not more tick surgery. (iii) Text still writes on genuine change — at speed the distance legitimately changes ~1–2×/s; that is the minimum a live banner can do, and it is now the ONLY invalidation the chrome ever takes. (iv) The reroute pill keeps its v128 state-flip gating and was not a blanking vector (it is hidden or shown as whole state); the planning screen's own banner is a different element under the Drive overlay and is untouched.
- **v134 — INVOICE + MANIFEST PRINT CLEANUP to the owner's WRITTEN SPEC + TEST PRINTS BURN NO NUMBER + the MARK-SETTLED DOOR. SHIPPED Oct 17, 2026.** **The failure (his screenshot, Oct 17 11:33 MDT): invoice-1153.pdf — an app print of the Oct 1–15, 2026 period — rendered COLLAPSED into running text: the hours header printed as the unseparated words "Description Rate Hours Amount", the data rows unaligned, and "Truck #07" welded to the table header as "Truck #07Hours", despite v122 having pinned a structured print as SOURCE. Root cause, proven in the code: on the owner's phone the print runs the NATIVE path (modules/invoice.js printDoc → MetPdf.savePdf({lines: MetPdf.htmlToLines(bodyHtml)}) in js/pdfexport.js), and the browser window the v122 pins described is not what his phone executes. htmlToLines' tokenizer knew h1|h2|p|div|table|tr but NOT h3, so the `<h3 class="sec">` section headings were never tokens; stripTags replaced every tag with '' (empty string), welding the text either side of a tag boundary into one word — the bill-to block's last line and the Hours heading became "Truck #07Hours" verbatim; a table row with more than 2 cells was flattened into ONE wrapped paragraph ({k:'p', a: cells.join(' ')}), so no columns and no rules could ever render; and div.totblock was ignored entirely, so the totals block had no separation on the native path even though the browser CSS drew one.** **The owner's spec (his own messages, treated as the spec verbatim): (1) more space between the "Bill to" block and the hours detail; (2) the hours belong in their OWN section, lined (ruled rows); (3) add a Pay period line; (4) everything under the Total becomes its own separate section — plus his Jul 2026 note on invoice #1150 ("Don't copy this exact! All the information on your invoice is good! It should be as detailed as that but! Structure!"), his Oct 16 direction that the app invoice look like the hand-built #1151, and his Oct 17 11:37 order "Same clean up on manifest."** **THE FIX — ONCE, AT THE SHARED LAYER (js/pdfexport.js; the invoice, the pay stub and every printDoc share it):** (i) the tokenizer now knows **h3**, so every `<h3 class="sec">` (Bill to / Hours / Notes, and the stub's Earnings / Deductions) becomes a real section-heading line with its rule and breathing room — spec (1); (ii) **stripTags turns block-level tags (br|div|p|h1–h4|table|tr|td|th|li|section) into a SPACE** before stripping the rest bare — inline tags (b, span, i) still vanish with no space, so emphasis split mid-word stays one word, and nothing can weld again; (iii) table rows keep their shape: cells are captured WITH their header-ness and right-alignment (class "tot" or text-align:right), and a row of **3+ cells becomes a 'cols' line**; (iv) the layout renders each run of 'cols' lines as a **REAL TABLE** — font 10, per-column widths computed from the widest cell + padding, ALL slack given to the first column (the Description column stretches — the #1151 shape), proportional shrink with a floor + in-column wrapping when content is too wide, the header row bold with a navy rule above AND below, a light rule under every data row, right-aligned cells anchored to their column's right edge, and the header repeated if a table crosses a page; the computed geometry is exposed (buildPdf().tables + MetPdf._textWidth) so tests pin the RENDER, not the markup — spec (2); (v) a **div.totblock becomes a new {k:'rule'} line** — a full-width navy rule — so everything under the Total stands as its own section on the native path exactly as the browser CSS already drew it — spec (4). The Pay period line (spec 3) was already in the markup (v105) and now actually survives the native pipeline as the date row's right-hand value. **1–2 cell rows are byte-identical to before** (kv / paragraph), so the pay stub's tables and the manifest-export §(b) pins are untouched, and the browser print path (real CSS, already structured) is unchanged. **MANIFEST (modules/manifest.js docLines):** the day's figures — Start/End, the tracker-source note, Odometer, Miles, Loads + source notes, any GPS loads block — now stand under their own **"Day summary"** section heading (printed only when at least one figure line under it will print), and the earned figure closes the section behind a rule as its totals row. The earned line ITSELF (kind, the v127 live/closed label — "Income earned today so far" while open / "Total Earned Income" once closed — and its value) is unchanged: capture-v127's pins pass with **NO pin move**. Content, order, section toggles and the on-screen preview are untouched. **MECHANISM FIX (a) — TEST PRINTS BURN NO NUMBER:** his words, Oct 17 11:36: "I am printing that period again and again to see its face!" — but "Save & print" WAS the issue path, so every look minted a fresh number (#1152, then #1153) and archived a duplicate record. Invoice now carries a **"🖨 Print preview — doesn't save"** button beside Save & print: it builds the print from the draft's current facts carrying the WOULD-BE number from the new pure helper **invCycWouldBeNo(list, typed, pref)** (typed wins, then the Preferences sequence, then max(issued)+1; reads nothing, writes nothing) and prints through the same printInvoice/printDoc. It never calls invIssue, never writes inv_list, never advances Preferences' next number, never clears the draft — pinned by source assertion in invoice-cycle-test (the preview handler's slice contains no invIssue(, no invW(, no setNextInvoiceNo) and by the lifecycle section (three would-be computations → list still empty, number unmoved; one issue → mints exactly the shown number, once). Re-printing an ISSUED invoice is unchanged: the archived record reprints with its own number. **MECHANISM FIX (b) — THE MARK-SETTLED DOOR:** his Oct 1–15 period was settled OUTSIDE the app (hand-built #1151, sent by him Oct 16; $3,000 paid, $610.50 outstanding) while the app still offered it as open — the root of the morning's confusion. A quiet card under Issued invoices — **"Settled outside the app?"** — takes a period (defaulting to the current open period) + the invoice number he used, behind an in-page two-step confirm (house pattern, no native confirm). **invCycMarkSettled** (INV-CYCLE region, verified write + read-back exactly like invIssue) stores the record IN inv_list flagged settledOutside with sub/tax/total **null — NO money figures are recorded or invented** — refuses a number already on record (collision, so a settled #1151 also blocks a future in-app #1151), advances the open period (his becomes Oct 16–31), and NEVER touches the Preferences next number (his stays 1152). The record renders in Issued invoices as a fact line (#1151 · Oct 1–15, 2026 · settled outside the app) with a remove door and **no total and no print link** (a null-total record can never reach the money render); **invCycRemoveSettled** removes only settledOutside records (verified; a real issued invoice is refused) and re-opens the period — fully reversible. **RENDER PROOF (the v122 lesson applied — the render was READ, not inferred):** the REAL #1151 record (invoice-format fixtures: Oct 1–15, 124.5 h, $3,610.50) was issued in-world and printed through the real printInvoice → real htmlToLines → real buildPdf, the PDF rasterized with pdftoppm and the page READ: business header + address; INVOICE #1151 ruled; Date with **Pay period: Oct 1–15, 2026** at the right; **Bill to** section — AI Trucking / 1506 16 St NE, Calgary, AB T2E 4T2 / **Truck #07, each on its own line**; **Hours** section — a real table: bold Description | Rate | Hours | Amount header between navy rules, 11 day rows aligned with a light rule under each, amounts right-aligned ($304.50…$333.50); **Notes** with the byte-for-byte generated note; a navy rule; then the totals as their own block (Net subtotal $3438.57 · GST (5%, included in the rate) — GST/HST 765662010 RT0001 $171.93 · Total due $3610.50 · Paid amount $0.00 · Balance owing $3610.50) and the Prepared-by footer. The MANIFEST likewise: a closed Oct 15 day rendered with MANIFEST title, Dispatches section, then the **Day summary** section (Start 8:30:00 AM / End 6:30:00 PM, the tracker-source honesty note, Odometer 168,759 -> 168,936 km (177 km)) closed by the rule and **Total Earned Income $333.50**. **CONTENT LAWS KEPT:** tax-INCLUSIVE totals (Total due = hours × rate exactly; Net = total − included GST; the GST line prints the GST number); the generated closing note byte-for-byte (REF_NOTE_1151 pin green); Bill-to prints the contracting company verbatim ("AI Trucking"); Truck # prints the stored value verbatim on its OWN line; Paid/Balance from invCycPaidTotal (honest $0.00 where no payment records exist); manifest content rules unchanged. **TESTS:** invoice-format **42 → 77** — a new rendered-structure section runs the real print end-to-end and pins the line shapes (h3 sections, standalone bill-to lines, NO welded text anywhere), the table (head + 11 rows, cell-by-cell first/last rows, only Amount right-aligned), the totals behind their rule with every figure, the built PDF's geometry (4 columns, xs strictly increasing from x=46, last column's right edge = 549 = the right margin), the content stream itself (day rows paint at the Description column x; amounts right-align to the Amount edge via _textWidth; ≥13 rule ops; header paints bold F2), and the 2-cell kv boundary the stub depends on. invoice-cycle **130 → 170** — the number lifecycle (would-be purity across three prints, mint-once on issue, reprint carries the archived number) and the mark-settled lifecycle (validation refusals record nothing; the record stored flagged + money-null; open period Oct 1–15 → Oct 16–31; Preferences advance NEVER reported by the door; the settled number collides both ways; removal refuses real invoices and re-opens the period) + statics for the door and the preview's purity. manifest-export **57 → 60** — the native-saved PDF's byte stream must contain "(Day summary)" BEFORE "(Start Time)" and "(Total Earned Income)"; its fail count stays EXACTLY 1 (the f1 baseline). paystub-auto **120/120** (the stub inherits the shared fix; its source pins are untouched — printInvoice/printPayStub markup was NOT changed: the collapse was in the pipeline, not the markup), capture-v127 **140/140**, money-truth 74/74, connect-live 83/83, connect-pipe 77/77, manifest-odo 31/31, manifest-times 19/19, manifest-toggles 49/49, navigator-v133 14/14. **FULL SUITE (63 suites + OCR harness 32/32): fail set = EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows** (background-day 38/1 clock fixture; chatbot 163/1 H2 stale-104 assert, now reading "got 134"; manifest-export 60/1 f1). webVersion 133 → 134; the 22 webVersion-cohort index pins moved to ?v=134; **DELIBERATE PIN MOVE: js/pdfexport.js ?v=96 → ?v=134** — its bytes changed in this build, and a stale cache pin on changed bytes defeats the pin's purpose (the remaining ?v=96 cohort and the ?v=102 pin are untouched); OTA manifest regenerated (46 files). Files changed: js/pdfexport.js, modules/invoice.js, modules/manifest.js, index.html, version.json, tools/invoice-format-test.js, tools/invoice-cycle-test.js, tools/manifest-export-test.js, BUGS.md. **RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly:** (i) The render proof is the app's OWN PDF engine executed in Node and rasterized with pdftoppm — the same bytes his phone's native path saves — not a screenshot from his WebView; per the USER-PROOF standard the verdict is his next test print of the Oct 1–15 invoice and a day manifest on v134. (ii) "Print preview — doesn't save" speaks of the app's records: like any print it still produces the PDF file itself (Documents/invoice-<number>.pdf via the native share/save flow); what it never does is archive the invoice in-app, mint/advance the number, or clear the draft. (iii) A settled-outside record is an in-app archive fact in inv_list (the same local list issued invoices live in); it records no payment state — the $610.50 still outstanding on #1151 remains a fact outside the app, and nothing in the door tracks it. (iv) Table geometry for very long invoices (multi-page) is pinned by the geometry assertions + the header-repeat code path, not by a multi-page fixture; the owner's periods (11–16 day rows) fit one page with room to spare.
- **v135 — DRIVE: TRUCK TRUTH (owner's field notes, Oct 17, 2026 12:05–12:11 MDT, from the recorded Ivor East Pond trips; evidence read frame-by-frame, `hidden_files/east-ivor-drive-recording-evidence.md` incl. §3 on the 11:44–11:56 long recording). SHIPPED Oct 17, 2026.** Five items — the four he ordered plus a fifth defect the long recording proved. **(1) NO U-TURNS, EVER — the safety item. PROVEN VERBATIM from his recording: the banner instructed "Make a left U-turn to stay on Bow Bottom Trail SE." (active 750 m → 30 m across ~2½ minutes, the drawn route doubling back in a hairpin), then "Make a left U-turn to stay on Canyon Meadows Drive SE." (240 m → 20 m); sound was ON, so both were VOICED too — and the frames show he muted voice guidance right after the U-turn stretches. His words: "This gps is for trucks! … It suggested me to make a uturn perhaps 5 times.. I knew what I was doing! Or else this could be troublesome!"** What the code audit found: the request side was already honest — `buildRouteRequest` has emitted `costing:'truck'` with the tandem profile all along (pinned B2) — but Valhalla's truck costing does not suppress U-turn maneuvers (types 12/13), and nothing downstream ever looked at them. THE FIX, TWO LAYERS, APP-SIDE ONLY (no server change — none was needed): *Layer 1, route around it* — `doRoute` (modules/navigator.js) now inspects every truck plan with the new NAVPURE `planUturns`; a plan carrying a U-turn is RE-REQUESTED with `exclude_locations` set to the turn-around points themselves (the new NAVPURE `pointAlongLine` computes each U-turn maneuver's start point on the decoded line; B5 pins the excluded point on the line at the maneuver's start ±40 m), so the next answer has to loop the block the way a truck actually drives. Bounded at two exclusion rounds; a failed round falls back to the route already in hand rather than an error banner (D1–D3). The REROUTE path rides the same function (`trackProgress → doRoute`), so a missed turn re-plans around the block too (E1–E6, driven through the real off-route trigger). *Layer 2, never present one* — if every answer still carries a U-turn, the new NAVPURE `sanitizeTruckPlan` rewrites it before the plan is stored: the instruction becomes the plain continuation onto the road it was turning onto ("Make a left U-turn to stay on Bow Bottom Trail SE." → "Continue on Bow Bottom Trail SE." — A13, the verbatim string), both spoken lines lose every U-turn phrase, the type becomes continue (8), distances untouched. Because the banner, the steps list, `drivePrompt`, `trackProgress` and `driveArrived` ALL read the one stored plan, the guarantee covers every channel — B8/C6/E5 assert across whole driven routes that NOTHING SPOKEN contains a U-turn, and C7 that the voice speaks the continuation instead. Car mode is untouched end-to-end (F1–F3: first answer taken, no truck costing, the engine's own U-turn words kept — a car can turn around). **(2) THE AVATAR IS A TRUCK.** His words: "this is a truck gps! An arrow as you! That's more like something puny! The moving avatar should be something bigger, a truck which you should be able to click and change!" The 30 px ▲ overlay is replaced by a ~52×82 px top-down TRUCK glyph (inline SVG, white-halo drop shadow, centred on the same 50%/64% anchor the plane spins under, so up on the glass is the direction of travel). Four kinds — dump truck (the default), semi, pickup, and the classic arrow for those who want it (`avatarGlyph`/`AVATAR_KINDS`, navigator.js). Tapping the marker (pointer-events:auto now) opens a compact chooser docked above the drive bar; a choice applies AT THE TAP and persists through the navigator settings store (`loadSettings`/`saveSettings` key 'navigator', new `avatar` field, default 'dump'). The drive tick never touches the node — G10 walks 25 ticks with the marker's innerHTML meter at zero (the v133 mutate-only guarantee stands; style changes happen on the tap, not the tick). **(3) THE MIC DOCKS TO THE SIDE.** His direction: "That should be on the side!" WHY v125's hide never worked, found in the code and now on the record in css/style.css: the only `mt-driving` rule ever written hid `#fbFab` — the FEEDBACK button. The button in every frame of his recordings (mid-map in landscape at ~69% across / ~68% down; sitting ON the ▶ Drive button on the long recording's post-arrival page) is the CHAT FAB, `#chatFab` (js/chatbot.js) — a different element no drive rule had ever governed, and its ordinary spot (bottom-right of the centred app column) IS that mid-map spot in landscape. THE MECHANISM FIX, not a re-asserted class rule: `syncFab` in js/chatbot.js now reads the Drive-open state itself — a new MutationObserver on `document.body` (the Navigator adds `mt-driving` in startDrive, removes it in exitDrive/teardown: the exact sequence his phone produces) — and while driving the FAB is FORCE-visible (even if #main drops 'active' underneath) and tagged `drive-dock`; the placement is CSS: `body.mt-driving #chatFab` pins it to the screen's RIGHT EDGE, vertically centred, compact 52 px, z-index 2100 (above the drive screen's 2000), safe-area aware, in both orientations; the chat sheet/scrim rise with it (2110/2120) so the docked mic actually opens the road hand over the drive face. Side-docking wins over the v125-era hide per his latest words — the road hand stays reachable, on the side. J1–J6 replay the phone's exact body-class sequence through the REAL buildUi and its real observers. **(4) FADING — his correction: "Not unflickering, it's fading." THE AUDIT, stated plainly: nothing app-side fades the Drive face.** Grep-proven over css/style.css (K7–K9): NO `transition` or `animation` on opacity exists anywhere in the stylesheet; the only `@keyframes` that touch opacity are `fade` (used by .step.on, .auth-land and a modal card — none of them Drive) and `syncpulse` (the sync dot); the drive plane's one transition is the spin's TRANSFORM .45s; the banner and bar carry no opacity rule at all in steady state; the drive tile layer has run `fadeAnimation:false` since v125 and is created with no opacity option (Leaflet default 1, static); `paintStep`'s `style.opacity` writes dim completed steps on the PLANNING screen only and are not animated. The v135 pins make the steady state explicit (`opacity:1` on .nv-drive-top/.nv-drive-bar — K8 counts exactly those two). What DOES fade a navigation screen mid-drive is the OS display timeout dimming the whole glass — and the app held NO wake lock for the Drive screen (the tracker's lock in js/miles.js belongs to the shift). THE FIX: Drive now holds a screen wake lock of its own — requested in startDrive, re-requested on foreground return (sentinels auto-release when the app hides), released in exitDrive and in teardown (H1–H6, against a stubbed wakeLock). HONEST CAVEATS: the 11:44–11:56 recording is PROVEN pre-v133 (one continuous session, no restart; v133 shipped mid-recording), so its blanking cluster documents the old face only; whether his 12:11 "fading" report came from that same un-restarted build or from an OS dim on the new one cannot be proven from here — the wake lock removes the app-controllable half either way, and if the face still fades on v135 in his hands, the remaining suspect is the WebView's own tile compositing, which no app CSS governs. **(5) PREMATURE "You have arrived" — NEW DEFECT from the long recording (bar still reading "Arrive · 3 min · 2.4 km left · to Ivor East Pond" at t=662 s; the session ended on "You have arrived" seconds later; final frames show the puck EAST of the Deerfoot roadway, off the route line, on the pin's side of the highway).** Mechanism proven in the code: `trackProgress` fired arrival on `haversineM(pos, dest) < 40` ALONE — pure straight-line proximity to the pin. The pond pin sits just off Deerfoot Trail, so passing it on the highway put the puck inside 40 m as the crow flies with 2.4 km of route remaining. THE FIX: arrival is now an ALONG-ROUTE fact — it fires only ON the line at its end (≤60 m off the line AND ≤30 m of route left), or AT the pin (<40 m) while in the route's final stretch (≤250 m of route left — the yard-at-the-end-of-the-road case, where the pin sits off the drawn line's end but the drive is genuinely done). Pinned with the owner's geometry as a synthetic out-and-back Valhalla response (I0–I4): puck on the outbound leg, 28 m straight-line from the pin, >2 km of route remaining → arrival must NOT fire (I2/I3); at the genuine end of the line it must (I4). **TESTS:** NEW tools/navigator-v135-test.js **84/84** (A: pure U-turn layer incl. the verbatim Bow Bottom string; B/C/D/E: real doRoute against U-turn fixtures — initial, insistence, failure, and reroute paths — steps, banner and every spoken line asserted U-turn-free, exclusion geometry asserted on the line; F: car mode; G: avatar; H: wake lock; I: arrival; J: the real chatbot buildUi under the phone's body-class sequence; K: static/CSS pins). navigator-test 179/179, navigator-v128 22/22, navigator-v133 14/14, navigator-screen 40/40, live-drive 68/68, chatbot 163/1 (the standing H2 row only). **FULL SUITE (64 suites + the new v135 suite + OCR harness 32/32): fail set = EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows** (background-day 38/1 clock fixture; chatbot 163/1 H2 stale-104 assert, now reading "got 135"; manifest-export 60/1 f1). webVersion 134 → 135; the 23 webVersion-cohort index pins moved to ?v=135 (no other pin moved — js/pdfexport.js was not touched this build and keeps its v134 pin); OTA manifest regenerated (46 files). Files changed: modules/navigator.js, js/chatbot.js, css/style.css, index.html, version.json, tools/navigator-v135-test.js, BUGS.md. NO server change (the guarantee is app-side by construction; Valhalla's exclude_locations is a documented /route parameter, and layer 2 holds the presentation guarantee even if an engine ignored it). **RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly:** (i) The exclusion round is proven against fixture responses shaped like the live engine's; whether the LIVE Valhalla honours exclude_locations for truck costing on his actual roads is proven only by his next drive — if it ignored them, layer 2 still guarantees he never SEES or HEARS a U-turn, but the drawn line could still double back until a U-turn-free answer arrives; the tell is the route line, and the fix then moves server-side with his word. (ii) The sanitized continuation keeps the engine's geometry — it changes the WORDS, not the drawn route; the around-the-block route comes from layer 1. (iii) The FAB dock is proven by replaying his phone's DOM/body-class sequence against the real code plus CSS pins — not on his glass; the WebView's fixed-position behaviour at the glass edge is the one untested surface. (iv) The wake lock assumes his WebView exposes navigator.wakeLock (guarded no-op where absent — Drive then behaves exactly as v134). (v) Per the USER-PROOF standard the verdict on all five items is his next drives: restart once to pull v135, then the Ivor run again — no U-turn offered in banner or voice, a truck under his thumb that he can change with a tap, the mic on the side, the face holding steady, and arrival only at the pond.
- **v136 — SYNC THAT NEVER SETTLES, FIXED IN THE ACCOUNTING (owner order Oct 17 12:20 MDT: "actively work on 2 — gps and this"; his evidence: the header dot "is not turning green anymore" and his 12:24 Preferences screenshot showing the Cloud sync card stuck on "Still syncing — give it a moment." with the dot amber).** CHARACTERIZATION FIRST (working-day replay against the v135 engine, measured): with the live writers running, the dot was green in **2 of 149 samples (1.3%)** and settled only ~8s after the writers STOPPED. PROVEN MECHANISM — two paths to eternal amber, one root (a single undifferentiated settle predicate): **(a) continuous writers (the brief's candidate (a))** — the old rule "green = no pushable key dirty at the instant refreshDot runs" is unreachable by construction during a working day: the telemetry stores `livemiles` (miles engine persist, ≤10s cadence) and `geoloads` (load counter persist, ≤10s on every raw fix, parked or driving) rewrite themselves all shift, and every rewrite re-dirties the queue and repaints amber instantly (store.set → refreshDot), while a multi-key flush over a mobile link regularly spans the next persist tick. Durable data DID reach the cloud all along — the flush drains it constantly — but green was gated on the telemetry churn draining too, and telemetry never drains. **(d) dead transport, masked (candidate (d))** — measured: with the Firebase auth object gone while CloudSync held the uid, 12s of waiting durable work produced amber forever with `lastError === null`: flush() early-returned silently and nothing ever named it. **(b) eliminated as the amber cause** — a rules-rejected store already painted RED with the key named (measured). **(c) accounting warts found en route** — a healthy PULL's success handler cleared `lastError` wholesale ("a healthy pull clears a stale earlier error", v34), wiping standing PUSH failures even though a pull pushes nothing; and no settle moment was recorded anywhere, so no face could say WHEN the last full backup happened. THE FIX (js/cloudsync.js; accounting + status only — **no record content, no data shape, no push/pull behaviour changes**): one truth machine `syncTruth()` paints the dot, `CloudSync.status()`, and the Preferences card. **GREEN** = every DURABLE key's latest state confirmed in the cloud; the moment is stamped in local sync meta (`lastSettledAt`, uid-scoped) and shown as "All synced · 12:41 PM" on the dot title, the card, and the tap-dot alert. **Telemetry keys (livemiles, geoloads) keep syncing through the same flush** — only their accounting is separated; status reports `telemetryBehind` honestly and the alert says "Live tracking is still catching up — your records are backed up." **AMBER** = genuinely in flight: a pull running, a flush carrying ≥1 durable key running, or durable edits waiting out their debounce (telemetry-only flushes no longer paint amber over a settled ledger). **NAMED FAILURE (red)** = a store whose push fails in ≥2 consecutive flushes (per-key failure books; the card names it in plain words — "a fuel entry hasn't backed up" — with the working Retry; a single blip retries quietly in amber instead of crying wolf), the sync service unreachable, repeated pull failures, or dead transport with durable work waiting — offline surfaces at once, unreachable-cloud after a 60s grace (auth restore on cold start is legitimate). Per-key records clear ONLY when that key actually pushes; a pull clears only pull failures (the v34 wart, fixed). The v131/v132 write paths are inside the accounting through their durable store keys (con_list/con_hidden pinned in the suite); their direct-Firestore request/response writes (directory/requests/shared-days) hold no queued local state and cannot wedge the indicator — no outbox invented, per the no-data-shape rule. FACES: modules/preferences.js — the sync note is now a live face of CloudSync.status() (painted on render + on every status change via the new `addStatusListener`), superseding the v107/v112 "feedback only after the Retry tap, no standing readout" rule for this one line BY THE OWNER'S Oct 17 ORDER (the card must show the same truth as the dot, with the settle time); the eternal-amber wording "Still syncing — give it a moment." is deleted. js/app.js — the tap-dot alert gains the settle time, the failing store in plain words, and the telemetry catch-up line. PROOF: NEW tools/cloudsync-v136-test.js **51/51** (real engine, controllable clock + transport: quiet settle + stamp at a known wall time + stamp advancing; the 20s working-day replay — green in 74/99 samples vs the measured 2/149 pre-fix, zero false failures, stamps advancing, the 6s/12s durable edits confirmed in the cloud MID-day while writers ran, telemetry pushes flowing throughout; telemetry-alone-behind miniature; poisoned fuel_list → named err + plain name + syncerr dot, healthy stores settle, healthy pull does NOT clear it, heal + retry → green; dead transport → grace-window syncing, then named blocked failure, restore → green with the waiting edit landed; offline → immediate named state → online → green; con_list/con_hidden durable-ledger pins; card/alert source pins). cloudsync-merge 14/14 unharmed. FULL SUITE (65 suites + OCR 32/32): fail set = EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows (background-day 38/1; chatbot 163/1 H2 stale assert, now reading "got 136"; manifest-export 60/1 f1). **NO pin moves beyond the standard cohort** (23 index pins ?v=135 → ?v=136; js/pdfexport.js untouched, keeps its v134 pin). webVersion 135 → 136, OTA manifest regenerated (46 files). Files changed: js/cloudsync.js, js/app.js, modules/preferences.js, index.html, version.json, tools/cloudsync-v136-test.js, BUGS.md. **RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly:** (i) The guarantee is proven against the real engine in the harness with the owner's real write shapes (livemiles/geoloads ≤10s persists, his durable keys) — the verdict per the USER-PROOF standard is his dot turning green on his phone DURING a working day; a restart pulls v136. (ii) The named-failure threshold (≥2 failed flushes) means a permanently rejected store shows amber for roughly one extra flush cycle (~seconds on a working phone, where telemetry writes re-arm flushes constantly) before it names itself — deliberate, so one network blip never cries red. (iii) `lastSettledAt` is stamped only by cycles this build observes: after the upgrade it first stamps at the first completed pull/flush (seconds after sign-in), never invented retroactively. (iv) The 60s dead-transport grace is a fixed window; on a phone whose auth restore legitimately takes longer after a cold start, the dot may name "can't reach the cloud" briefly and then clear itself when the flush runs — the retry button also clears it by succeeding.
- **v139 — DRIVE IMAGE BREAKING: THE ARCHITECTURE REPLACED, NOT A SIXTH LAYER (owner verdict on the v135 road test, Oct 17, 2026 12:48 MDT: "This image breaking is getting worst on every build! fix it" — and 12:42: "Instead of saying sound on and off! It should give an option to mute!"; the truck art verdict 12:46: "This truck looks horrible… better 3d image of a truck! Still cartoonish but should have allot more details then a 2d image slapped on"). SHIPPED Oct 17, 2026.** **THE FRAME EVIDENCE (his ~74 s recording on v135, read frame-by-frame):** brief PERFECT frames over an unchanged DOM alternate with the navy banner painted displaced ~+40% right / −6% up, half off-screen (only "on 2/Deerfoot Trail" legible); the ETA bar displaced by the SAME vector (its left half — the arrival stack — off-screen, buttons only); grey unloaded tile wedges sweeping the plane; the blue route line reduced to stubs; at ~55 s the bar AND the docked mic gone entirely; then the banner back, clipped mid-word at ~55% width. **DIAGNOSIS (defensible from the code + the frames):** layout and JS writes are ruled out — the DOM is unchanged between the perfect and broken frames, two independent sibling layers share ONE displacement vector, and v133's write-on-change meters (still green in this build) prove the text writes were never the paint failure. What the frames show is the Android WebView compositor misplacing, clipping and dropping layers under load, and the load was architectural: the map box was 340% × 340% of the viewport (≈1224 × 2176 CSS px ≈ 3700 × 6500 device px at his DPR — ≈24M device px, its height beyond the 4096 max-texture dimension of his phone class's GPUs) wrapped in THREE nested 3D contexts (a perspective parent, a static rotateX(52°) tilt layer, and a will-change spin layer whose .45s transition re-rasterized through every turn), with Leaflet's own transformed panes inside — while the truck marker sat INSIDE the perspective context carrying a transform + a drop-shadow filter, and the banner/bar rode as v133-promoted layers (translateZ(0) + will-change) in the same starved compositor. The build history is the confession: v124 enlarged the plane, v125 enlarged it again (300%→340%), v128 gated the writes, v133 promoted the chrome, v135 added the docked mic as another transformed element — every build added compositor load or layers, and the breaking worsened every build, exactly as the owner said. **THE FIX (css/style.css + modules/navigator.js; presentation only — every v135 behaviour is untouched):** (1) **ONE transformed element on the whole drive screen.** The tilt/spin/mapbox stack is deleted. In its place `.nv-drive-plane`: 190% × 190% of the viewport (≈3.2× less raster than 340%²; ≈2050 × 3650 device px at DPR 3 — under the texture ceiling on both axes), positioned so its centre sits exactly on the driver anchor (left:-45%, top:-31% = 50−95, 64−95), transform-origin at that centre, carrying the Leaflet map as its only child. The drive tick writes its ONE composed transform — `perspective(1100px) rotateX(45deg) rotate(−heading)` (the same geometry the nested tilt∘spin produced: in-plane spin first, pitch around the screen axis through the anchor, projection vanishing at the anchor) — only on the v128 ≥2° heading deadband, with NO transition, NO will-change, NO backface-visibility, and no perspective PROPERTY anywhere (the viewport is a plain overflow:hidden box again; its sky gradient remains the backstop for any residual sliver, its v107 job). The plane is painted from the first tick (north-up until a heading exists) instead of sitting flat until the first course fix. **TILT CONCEDED, PLAINLY: 52° → 45°** — at bounded size the steeper pitch's far reach (0.64·H/cos52° ≈ 665 px on a 640 px-tall phone) outruns any plane that fits the GPU (190% gives 608 px of half-height; the 45° need is 580 px, covered in portrait AND landscape per the math pinned in the suite); 45° is the bottom of the standard 45–60° navigation band, and where a gorgeous tilt and a stable image conflicted on this GPU class, STABILITY WON — the owner was promised the fix goes at the tilted map itself, and it did. (2) **THE CHROME CANNOT SHEAR.** Banner, ETA bar, marker, avatar chooser, reroute pill: all are now direct children of the `.nv-drive` root (marker/chooser/pill moved OUT of the viewport element), plain absolutely-positioned boxes carrying NO transform, NO will-change, NO transition, NO filter. Centring is done without transforms: the marker by negative margins from a per-kind box table (AVATAR_BOX), the chooser/pill by left:0;right:0 + auto margins, the docked chat FAB by arithmetic (`top:calc(50% - 26px)` for the 52 px button — its translateY(−50%) is deleted; the dock's behaviour, sheet and scrim are unchanged). A box with no transform and no promotion has exactly one painted position — its layout position; there is no second position for a compositor to pick. The v133 `opacity:1` steady-state pins (K8) still hold — the chrome's opacity declarations are the only ones on the screen. (3) **MUTE BUTTON (the 12:42 catch).** The drive bar's "🔊 Sound: on / 🔇 Sound: off" TEXT button is now a proper mute control: an inline-SVG speaker — sound waves when live; an × plus a bold orange slash and a warning-tint ground (`.muted`, colour only) when muted — readable at a glance from across the cab, with aria-pressed/aria-label tracking the state. The state is the SAME `settings.muted` as ever (it already persisted across drives — his frames showed "Sound: off" held — and it still does; speak() still gates on it; muting still cancels speech). The paint is write-on-change (a painted-state cache), never per tick. The PLANNING card keeps its text toggle on the same setting (a settings row, read parked). (4) **TRUCK ART (the 12:46 catch).** All four chooser kinds redrawn as detailed, dimensional inline SVG: gradient-shaded cab (glass-gradient windshield, roof panel, mirrors, chrome bumper, headlights, amber roof beacon), a steel dump box with crowned gradient, recessed bed, rim light and ribs plus a tailgate bar (dump), a corrugated silver trailer with rear-door cross and trailer tandems (semi), a cab + recessed open bed (pickup), a gradient chevron with white stroke (arrow); wheels are two-tone (dark tyre + hub) — six on the dump, eight on the semi; the soft ground shadow is baked into the art (the marker's drop-shadow filter is gone with the transforms). Gradient ids are unique per kind AND per instance (marker vs picker), viewBoxes match AVATAR_BOX exactly, all eight instances XML-validated and raster-proofed in the build. The chooser, tap-to-change and persistence are unchanged, and the tick still never touches the marker. **PROOF:** NEW tools/navigator-v139-test.js **54/54** — structural pins (old layers absent from CSS AND markup; plane geometry + the anchor arithmetic; raster ratio < 0.32 of the v135 plane; the viewport composition string proving the plane is the viewport's ONLY child; exactly ONE style.transform writer in navigator.js and it writes the composed string under the ≥2° gate; the LAYER BUDGET asserted across every drive-screen rule — zero will-change/translateZ/preserve-3d/backface-visibility anywhere, zero transform declarations outside the plane's transform-origin; chrome rules individually transform-free; the dock arithmetic) + behaviour on the real module (instrumented plane-style write counting: painted at drive open at heading 40, ONE write per real turn, ZERO on 1° jitter; ten ticks never repaint the marker; the mute button's icon/class/aria/persistence/silence end-to-end and its fresh-render paint from a muted store; the chooser's swap + per-kind box/margins + gradient-id uniqueness; persisted avatar painting sized in a fresh render). **DELIBERATE PIN MOVES, each cited in-suite:** navigator-test (element registry nvd_spin→nvd_plane; the four heading/deadband assertions now read the composed plane transform; the v101 52° CSS-tilt pin → the v139 45° composed-transform pin); navigator-screen-test (340% mapbox / viewport-perspective / 52° pins → the bounded-plane pins); navigator-v133-test (registry; the three closing STRUCTURE pins — chrome promotion + tilt will-change — replaced by their v139 supersession pins; the suite's driveText BEHAVIOUR pins bind unchanged); navigator-v135-test (registry; K1 dock translateY pin → arithmetic-centring pin; K6 marker 52px/translate pin → AVATAR_BOX pin; G2/G7/G9 glyph signatures re-keyed from the flat art's colours to the dimensional art's gradient ids). All other v135 guarantees re-verified green untouched: no U-turns in any channel, truck costing + exclusion re-requests, along-route arrival, retracting line, ETA stack, dock behaviour, wake lock, avatar chooser/persistence, K7/K8/K9 opacity discipline. **FULL SUITE (66 suites + OCR):** fail set = EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows (background-day 38/1; chatbot 163/1 H2 stale assert, now reading "got 139"; manifest-export 60/1 f1). **RESIDUAL LIMIT, stated honestly: on-glass stability is proven ONLY by the owner's next drive recording.** This suite pins the structure and the write behaviour in a DOM harness; the compositor that tore his frames is his phone's WebView GPU and cannot run in the build environment. What this build can promise structurally: the oversized multi-context plane is gone, the transformed-element count on the screen is one, the chrome has no transform or promotion left to misplace, and the raster load is a third of what broke. If tearing still appears on his glass, the next lever is shrinking the plane further toward 150% (accepting sky-gradient corners at extreme headings) — not another layer. webVersion 136 → 139 (137/138 are the queued Preferences and Road-hand builds; the number follows the owner's queue order). What renders now is only the signed-in user's OWN money identity: an owner-operator sees "Client billing rate ($/foreman hour)" (placeholder "Blank = your wage rate") + "My wage rate ($/hour)"; a pure driver sees ONE field, "Your pay rate ($/hour)"; an office/boss face (role office OR account type Fleet Manager) renders NO rate fields at all — crew pay rates live in each driver's P&L entry, which was already the v132 pipe's only wage source and is untouched. Don't need to be there!"; 12:26, of the server text box: "If that is altered app..").** The Power Read card AND the Navigator server card are removed from the Advanced view ENTIRELY — markup and wiring both (not greyed, not moved). The Navigator server address is now a BAKED-IN CONSTANT: modules/navigator.js loadSettings() returns NAV_DEFAULT_SERVER_URL ('https://2.31.47.19', the verified production address) UNCONDITIONALLY — a stored address from an older build (custom or deliberately cleared '') is ignored, and saveSettings() refuses to persist a serverUrl patch. the other 24 were code comments, scrubbed too (rewritten to "the owner"), so the new suite's grep is absolute: NO /\bAsjad\b/ in modules/*.js, js/*.js or index.html. **TESTS:** NEW tools/preferences-v137-test.js **112/112** — rendered faces for all four role worlds in a rendered-id-aware fake DOM (OO two fields showing $29;
