- **v138 — ROAD HAND EARS (owner's hands-on catches Oct 17, 2026, 12:31–12:37 MDT; ships as webVersion 141 — the OTA number only moves forward).** Three named items, one build, js/chatbot.js + tools/chatbot-v141-test.js (new) + index.html cohort pins + version.json + this entry only. **CHARACTERIZATION FIRST (current core replayed in Node against his exact exchange):** a full-facts first message already computed (3 holes FORWARD); the measured failures were the three he hit — an amount-only message ("I'm 800 over") was answered with the combined question re-asking the amount he had JUST given; his spoken "Drivea" left heavy=null and the IDENTICAL combined question came back verbatim on the 2nd and 3rd asks; and "Why is this app not syncing?" routed 'general', so the glue's pending-flow absorb swallowed it as a slot answer and the axle question repeated. **(a) MIC (12:31 video — tapping the mic does nothing).** Root cause in code: the old listen() knew ONE route — web SpeechRecognition — which the Android WebView does not genuinely provide, and its onerror handler swallowed every failure and silently reset the icon, so a dead recognizer presented as a dead button. No native speech plugin exists in the shell-facing code (Capacitor.Plugins carries only Filesystem, BackgroundGeolocation, ShiftTrack, LocalNotifications — no SpeechRecognition bridge in APK v35). The chain now runs in the briefed preference order: (1) a native Capacitor SpeechRecognition plugin if the shell ever ships one (defensive startListening/requestPermission handling, failure falls through); (2) record-and-transcribe through the Power Read AI machinery — MediaRecorder + getUserMedia capture, the clip transcribed by the same Gemini generateContent chain (MODELS/ENDPOINT) the general brain's fallback already rides, gated on the pre-existing Power Read key (transcribeKey) — still NO key field, prompt, or errand anywhere in this feature; (3) web SpeechRecognition where the platform genuinely has it — hardened: maxAlternatives 3 with the first non-empty transcript winning, no-speech/network errors speaking their own lines, and fatal errors (not-allowed / service-not-allowed / audio-capture — the WebView case) falling through to the recorder ONCE instead of dying silently; the recorder cross-falls-back to the recognizer when capture is unavailable/denied; (4) every remaining failure lands in micSay — a clear spoken-style card, the icon restored, typing untouched. Tap-while-listening stops. closeSheet stops any live capture. **(b) UNDERSTANDING.** Core: new fuzzyGroup() — the axle-group lexicon matched exactly or one edit away ("Drivea"→drives, "drivs"→drives, "trailor"→trailer), applied to short bare answers and to the word after "heavy on"/"over on" (so "heavy on my drivea" and "800 over on the drives" land in ONE shot). mathStep keeps miss bookkeeping (st.misses/st.lastHeard — a reply that lands nothing new while a slot is awaited): a miss says what it heard ("I heard “banana” — …"), rewords the ask, and for the group returns tappable choices (GROUP_CHOICES) that the glue renders as buttons ON the card — tapping one feeds ask() and resolves like the spoken word. Never-re-ask: with the amount in hand the follow-up is the group-only question; with the group in hand the amount ask stands as before; existing first-ask strings are byte-identical (the pinned C5–C9 conversation in chatbot-test passes unchanged). Context (groups/excess/per-hole/kind) keeps accumulating across the whole flow exactly as before, now including garbled answers. **(c) TOPIC CHANGE.** Intent detection now runs on EVERY message before slot consumption: CORE.route() gains a 'sync' layer (sync/syncing/synced/backed up/backing up), and CORE.isSlotAnswer() decides whether a message can be a pending answer at all (a fact, a number, a group — exact or garbled — or a short fragment; a complete question never qualifies). In ask(), a non-math message that isn't a slot answer abandons pendingMath instantly and is answered on its own topic. The sync question is answered from the app's REAL sync status: answerSync() reads CloudSync.status() (the v136 truth machine) and CORE.syncText() composes the words — ok (with the last-settled time + an honest telemetry-behind note), syncing, err (offline named; a stuck store named via failPlain with the Preferences Retry door; unreachable-cloud wording), off (not signed in), and a no-status fallback that teaches the dot instead of guessing. The general-knowledge brain's offline state is untouched — nothing is faked. **TESTS:** NEW tools/chatbot-v141-test.js **79/79** — Part A replays his exchange against the real core (one-shot answer, group-only follow-up, Drivea/drivs/trailor/"heavy on my drivea" resolutions, miss → heard + choices + no verbatim repeat, choice taps complete, amount-miss rewording, per-hole carry); Part B pins route/isSlotAnswer/syncText over the v136 status shape; Parts C–E drive the REAL glue in fake-DOM worlds — his phone's shape (no recognizer, no key: mic tap → graceful spoken line + icon restored; the mid-flow curveball gets the sync answer with the stubbed CloudSync settle time and the axle question is never re-asked; the orphan group answer afterwards falls to the general layer, proving the flow was abandoned; miss choices render on the card and a tap completes the slide), the recorder world (fake getUserMedia/MediaRecorder/FileReader + a Gemini-shaped fetch: tap → 🔴 → tap → the transcript lands as his own message and is answered), and the broken-recognizer world (service-not-allowed → fall-through → spoken line, icon restored); Part F statics pin the listen() chain order, the intent gate ahead of the absorb, and the sync branch. chatbot-test 163/1 — the SAME standing H2 row (stale-104 assert, now reading "got 141" after the bump — name/set unchanged, deliberately not fixed: it is one of the 3 baseline rows). **NO pin moves anywhere; NO other suite touched.** webVersion 140 → 141; the 23 index pins moved to ?v=141; OTA manifest regenerated. **RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly:** (i) The mic's routes 1–2 cannot be proven from the build environment — whether the v35 native shell grants the WebView's audio-capture (getUserMedia) request is a native-side fact this web build cannot see, and route 2 additionally needs the pre-existing Power Read key present on the phone (the owner's Power Read runs through the server proxy, which carries text/images, not audio — a server-side /ai/transcribe endpoint is a server change, outside this build's scope, and is the named next lever if his tap shows the recorder cannot run). What IS proven: on a phone with no working route the button now answers with a clear spoken line instead of silence, and every working-route combination is harness-proven end to end. The verdict is his next mic tap, as always. (ii) Fuzzy matching is deliberately conservative (one edit, group lexicon only): a garble two edits away lands in the miss path — heard back to him with tappable choices — rather than being guessed at. (iii) The topic-change gate abandons a pending weights flow for any complete new intent (his legislated behaviour); a driver who asks an unrelated question mid-flow and then answers the old slot will restart the weights talk from his next math message — facts he already stated in earlier messages are not carried across the abandonment, by design of the gate he tested for.
- **v140 — LOAD AUTO-COUNT (owner's law, restated Oct 17, 2026: load counts are NEVER the driver's job; ships as webVersion 142 — the OTA number only moves forward).** On Oct 17 he did at least 3–4 loads by 11:20 MDT while the app recorded none of them automatically — the only counter was the manual "+1 load" dashboard button (dashboard 12:17: "+1 load · 5 today", all manual). **CHARACTERIZATION FIRST (the shipped glue replayed in Node on the day's real pair — Aecon Maple Ridge load pin 50.96044,-114.03176 <-> Ivor East Pond, the dispatch carrying ONLY the load pin, exactly as saved):** in clean traces with 10 s fixes the engine counted correctly when tip dwells ran >= ~150 s and recorded ZERO all day below that (the day sat in 'learning' forever); with 60 s fix cadence and 300 s tips it also counted 4/4. So the engine was fragile, not dead — and three structural gaps, each proven in code, guaranteed the user-facing failure: **(1)** the dashboard NEVER displayed the GPS count — paintTrackerExtra rendered only trkAccum().manualLoads (the taps), and the shared dayLoads ladder ranked the geo count LAST (below taps), so even a perfect count was invisible where he looks and outranked by any single tap. **(2)** The dump fence could only come from the dispatch's dumpLat/dumpLng — a field NOTHING in the app populates for real dispatches (grep-proven: only geoloads' own one-shot geocode writes it, and that path requires a street-address dropoff; looksLikeAddress deliberately refuses site names) — or a remembered pair, or same-day teaching. Yet the app already KNEW both sites: Maple Ridge and Ivor East Pond sit in his own work favorites (v91 migration, nav_data.work) and the 9 built-in pits ship in navigation — and js/geoloads.js never consulted the places data at all. A leftover pinless second dispatch record that day could not resolve even a load fence (its jobSite name was never matched against places). **(3)** Teaching was brittle at real tip behaviour: the candidate cluster hung off its FIRST fix, so GPS wander restarted the 3-minute dwell clock mid-stop. **THE BUILD (js/geoloads.js, modules/invoice.js, modules/manifest.js, modules/dispatch.js, js/app.js, css/style.css):** (a) **Places-aware resolution** — a dispatch with no load pin resolves its load site BY NAME against the known places (own work favorites + the built-in pits via Meticulous.modules.navigation.builtinPlaces()): exact match first, containment second, UNIQUE match only — two places answering to the name resolves nothing ('Aecon' -> no fence; a fence is never placed on a guess). (b) **The navigated hand-off** — the day's dump resolution gains the signal the app already records: nav_data.recent (written by Navigation at the Navigate TAP, before the drive). While a load site is known and no dump is resolved, the newest work navigation from today (same calendar day, not hours before the session) that forms a valid pair with the load site is ADOPTED as the day's dump (src 'navigated', day record carries navDump): the counter arms BEFORE he arrives, so even a 90-second tip counts. Navigating to the load site itself adopts nothing (pairing rule). Adoption never overrides a pin/pair/taught dump. Two subtleties found by the new suite and fixed in-build: he navigates when LEAVING the pit loaded, so adoption lands mid-loaded-leg — the counter is seeded armed from the learner's confirmed leg at adoption, and re-checked lazily in onFix (the adoption can land inside the learner's 45 s departure-confirm window; the first fix after the leg stands confirmed re-seeds the still-untouched counter armed) — and the observe learner inherits the teach learner's leg snapshot instead of forgetting it. The pair is remembered (geoload_pairs) only once a cycle is PROVEN at the adopted site (first counted dump arrival), the same standard teaching is held to. (c) **Teaching hardened, still the last resort** — the cluster rides its running CENTROID (wander no longer resets the clock), dwell bar 3 min -> 2 min (a real tip: position + raise + lower), cluster radius 75 -> 100 m. Measured sensitivity in the replay geometry (10 s fixes, ~1.9 km legs): a 25 s drive-through teaches nothing (count 0, blocked 'learning' — honest); a 45 s tip teaches (approach presence at the place counts toward the 2 minutes — the learner has never had a speed gate; presence is the signal). The contest/ambiguity rules are untouched. (d) **The count becomes the count of record** — NEW GeoLoads.today() ({count, blocked, sites, armed} — live state while watching, the persisted record otherwise); the dashboard shows "🚛 Loads today: N" with "counted by GPS" while a pair is fenced, and the v82 button is DEMOTED to a small "+1 load correction" beneath it (id/handler/bookkeeping unchanged — manualLoads still accumulates as the correction; while no pair is fenced yet, the correction count is what shows, honestly). The shared dayLoads ladder moves 'sites' ABOVE 'taps' (tickets -> recorded -> sites -> taps -> weight); the manifest's no-helper fallback ladder is aligned; the dispatch close pre-fill takes the machine count first, taps second (the ticket's own loads still win; the confirm field stays his override). **TESTS:** NEW tools/geoloads-v140-test.js **38/38** — (A) the Oct 17 replay WITH his navigation (pit navigation adopts nothing; pond navigation arms the counter before arrival; four 90 s-tip cycles count EXACTLY 4; the pair is remembered once proven; dashboard today() == forDate == the stored record the manifest reads), (B) teaching at 90 s tips counts 3/3 with the learned source named; three 25 s drive-throughs count 0 with blocked 'learning', (C) a pinless 'Maple Ridge' dispatch counts 2/2 (load src 'places', dump src 'navigated'); jobSite 'Aecon' resolves nothing ('no-site-coords'), (D) a 40-minute fix blackout invents nothing (2 observed, blackout, +1 observed = 3), (E) statics pin the ladder order, the dashboard face, the close pre-fill order. money-truth 74 -> 75: **DELIBERATE PIN MOVE, cited in-suite** — the v107 case "untouched form default + taps 3 + geo 4" pinned taps-over-geo; it now expects the machine count ({4, 'sites'}) under the owner's law, and a new case pins taps as the correction when the machine has nothing. geofence-loads 58/58 with **two cited pin moves** (the teaching-boundary checks moved from the ~3 min bar to the ~2 min bar; the snapshot/restore dwell segments re-sized to the new bar). chatbot-v141 F10 ship-version pin moved 141 -> 142 per the standing convention. **FULL SUITE (69 suites + OCR 32/32): fail set = EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows** (background-day 38/1; chatbot 163/1 H2 stale assert; manifest-export 60/1 f1 clock fixture) — verified against a pre-change baseline run; per-suite counts otherwise identical except money-truth +1 (new case) and the new suite. webVersion 141 -> 142; the 23 index pins moved to ?v=142 and js/geoloads.js's pin moved ?v=96 -> ?v=142 (its first change since v96); OTA manifest regenerated (46 files). Files changed: js/geoloads.js, js/app.js, css/style.css, modules/invoice.js, modules/manifest.js, modules/dispatch.js, index.html, version.json, tools/geoloads-v140-test.js (new), tools/money-truth-test.js, tools/geofence-loads-test.js, tools/chatbot-v141-test.js, BUGS.md. **RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly:** (i) A loader working >120 m from the site pin still can't confirm the load visit (the fence is the fence) — mitigated by name resolution and navigation, not solved; a pit whose pin sits at the gate while the loader works deep inside remains a same-family risk. (ii) Teaching's presence rule can teach on a genuine >= 2-minute stop on a loaded leg (a fuel stop): the contest rule, the ambiguity retract, and the close-confirm override are the guards, as they were in v68 — and a WRONG navigated adoption (he navigates somewhere else first) fences the wrong dump and counts 0, visibly, in the day record; nothing is invented either way. (iii) The pre-session first cycle (he loaded before the 8:00 AM dispatch-anchored session start, Oct 17 shape) rides the native crumb drain's session-start-minus-60 s cutoff — bounded, pre-existing, not this build's mechanism. (iv) The verdict per the USER-PROOF standard is his next working day: dashboard count vs his own tally at close (restart pulls 142; counting needs the session running and LiveMiles fixes flowing, as before).
- **v141 — TRUCK ART REDO: THE ART IS A RENDER NOW, NOT A DRAWING (owner verdict on the v139 art, Oct 17, 2026 13:35 MDT, with a live Drive screenshot: "Still horrendous! When I said 3d! That what came to mind? When I said realistic is this it?"; ships as webVersion 143 — the OTA number only moves forward).** The Drive position marker's truck has now been rejected TWICE: v135's flat glyph ("better 3d image of a truck! Still cartoonish but should have allot more details then a 2d image slapped on") and v139's dimensional inline-SVG redraw, which still read as a flat cartoon on his glass. THE FIX CHANGES MEDIUM, NOT DETAILS: each vehicle kind is now a PHOTOREALISTIC TOP-DOWN 3D RENDER shipped as a PNG asset (`assets/avatar-dump.png` / `avatar-semi.png` / `avatar-pickup.png`) — a glossy navy cab with sky reflected in the glass, a steel dump box carrying a real gravel load (dump), a corrugated trailer (semi), an open bed (pickup); treaded tyres, mirrors, chrome tanks, and a soft ground shadow baked into the raster. HOW PRODUCED: the three renders were AI-generated on a flat chroma-green ground (strict top-down, pointing up), then keyed to transparency with a despill pass, the ground shadow composited under each truck, and sized at 3× the marker box (`tools/` working pipeline in the build's scratch files; the assets are the deliverable). RASTER-PROOF GATE: the finished assets were rendered at the EXACT marker size over a street-like ground and read as an image before shipping — at 62 px wide they read as little real trucks (glass, load, trailer corrugation all survive); a cartoon read would not have shipped. The renders carry true vehicle proportions (aspect ~2.4–4.8 — the semi's trailer dominates, as it should); `AVATAR_BOX` grows to fit them (dump 62×118, semi 62×136, pickup 60×104; arrow unchanged 54×72) and compresses the length the way every navigation marker does. 'arrow' KEEPS its v139 chevron SVG — a symbol, never the complaint. NOT REGRESSED (suite-pinned): the marker still centres by negative margins off AVATAR_BOX (no transforms added — the new CSS is two plain sizing rules, `.nv-drive-you img` / `.nv-avatar-opt img`); the v139 single-transform plane, chrome, mute button, no-U-turn and arrival logic are untouched; the chooser (dump default / semi / pickup / arrow), the tap-to-change and the settings persistence work exactly as before, now swapping the render + box at the tap; the drive tick still never repaints the marker (v133/v139 mutate-only, measured in the new suite across 10 ticks). TESTS: new `tools/navigator-v141-test.js` 36/36 (asset wiring, the assets themselves decoded and alpha-probed — keyed corners transparent, truck opaque at centre, exactly 3× box — CSS budget, chooser swaps for pickup AND arrow, persistence across a fresh render, no-tick-repaint); deliberate pin moves, each cited in place: navigator-v139 (the §4 art pins moved from gradient/wheel signatures to the asset wiring; box sizes in §5/§7/§8), navigator-v135 (G1/G2/G7/G9 glyph signatures moved to the render assets), chatbot-v141 F10 (142→143, the standing ship-pin convention). Full suite: 70 suites + OCR 32/32, fail set EXACTLY the 3 standing baseline rows (background-day clock fixture 38/1; chatbot H2 stale webVersion-104 assert 163/1; manifest-export f1 fixture 60/1). THE HONEST LIMIT: the art is a RENDER (AI-generated raster, keyed and composited) — no suite can judge realism; it was proofed at marker size by eye in this build, and the verdict that counts is the owner's eyes on his next drive. Scope: `modules/navigator.js` (avatar block only) + `css/style.css` (two rules) + 3 new assets + tests + version/manifest + this entry. ONE local commit, pushed via gh-push-api.py as a single HEAD~1..HEAD diff; live per-file hashes verified against version.json after deploy.
- **v142 — POWER READ SPEED (owner's catch, Oct 17, 2026, 14:41 MDT: "And scanning and then power read usually is taking a few minutes, no body has that much time before leaving to get to a job site on their way there or when they get back!"; ships as webVersion 144).** Pairs with his rule from the same hour: the odometer enters ONLY from the dash photo — a photo-only door that takes minutes is a door nobody uses. **CHARACTERIZATION FIRST, all measured on his real fixtures against the live endpoint (Oct 17):** (1) THE SERIAL PIPELINE WAS THE MINUTES. Every surface ran the local Tesseract read to completion before the cloud request was even sent. Local timings (Node tesseract.js, desktop-class CPU — a phone is slower): the dash photo took 23.4 s at full camera resolution (2048px — the Preferences/Maintenance wiring recognized the RAW frame), 8.9 s at the 1600px OCR rendition, 2.8 s at 1280, 1.4 s at 1024 — and at NO size did Tesseract find the dot-matrix odometer digits (0 numbers in 4 sizes), so on his dash the local leg is pure wait before an inevitable escalation. The Petro receipt is the opposite: local read cracks it fully at 1600px (4.9 s) but loses the money column at 1280 — so the local rendition stays 1600 and only the SEND shrinks. (2) THE CLOUD READ ITSELF IS FAST: odometer at 1600px (727 KB body) 2.5–4.4 s; at 1280 (477 KB) 2.1–2.8 s; at 1024 (307 KB) 1.5–2.3 s — correct (169,272) on every success. Receipt transcription at 1600 (542 KB) 5.0 s; at 1280 (363 KB) 4.5–5.8 s — every money figure correct both runs. (3) THE PROXY ROUTE HAD NO CLIENT TIMEOUT (`callProxy` had no AbortController at all): a slow server-side model chain-walk (45 s/attempt server-side) could hold the spinner indefinitely; the direct route had 45 s. (4) THE v118 RETRY DOUBLED SERIALLY: one transient failure = +1.2 s + a whole second attempt, still after the local wait. (5) ~1 in 3 probe calls returned 502 brain_unavailable in bursts (free-tier throttling; recovered in ~a minute) — server-side, out of this build's scope, named as a residual below. **THE BUILD — the race, on every surface, through the shared path:** (a) `PowerRead.beginEarly({reader, image, origin, onSettled})` (js/powerread.js) starts the cloud read THE MOMENT the photo exists and registers it under (reader, photo); `PowerRead.escalate` ADOPTS a matching early read — its outcome feeds the existing delivery, no second send (dedupe pinned: one photo = one fetch, even with two beginEarly calls + an escalation). One shared outcome shaper (`settleRead`) now serves both entries: 'done' (through the same v80 two-sided odometer plausibility gate — an implausible cloud value can never settle 'done'), 'waiting' (network-class → the photo joins the offline queue exactly once), 'implausible', 'error'. An early result nobody adopts is deliberately NOT stashed into powerReadResults (that store is photo-unbound; a transcription landing in a different surface's form would be worse than losing it) — the queue drain remains its only producer. A synchronously-throwing/rejecting transport settles as network-class, never an unhandled rejection. (b) WAIT BOUNDS: one shared per-attempt ceiling `FETCH_TIMEOUT_MS = 20000` (the proxy route gains the AbortController it never had; the direct route's hard-coded 45 s routes through the same constant), the v118 retry keeps its single retry but at a shorter `RETRY_TIMEOUT_MS = 12000` — worst case ≈ 33 s in the BACKGROUND, and a hung fetch aborts into the honest 'waiting' state instead of minutes of spinner (pinned end-to-end with manual timers: abort at 20 s → retry at 12 s → 'waiting', photo queued once, exactly 2 fetches, never a third). (c) SEND SIZE: `TicketOCR.READ_MAX` = odometer 1024px / text 1280px / sticker 1280px (accuracy proven above at exactly those sizes on the real fixtures); `fileToReadImage` / `urlToReadImage` build the send rendition; request bodies drop 727→307 KB (dash) and 542→363 KB (receipts). (d) THE LOCAL READ STARTS IMMEDIATELY AND NEVER ON A RAW FRAME: `TicketOCR.recognize` now caps any data-URL input to the 1600px OCR rendition internally (`_capReadSize`; File/Blob inputs pass through, non-DOM environments degrade to passthrough), so the one caller still handing it a camera frame (the photo wiring) is capped centrally and no surface waits on a rendition build before its local read starts. (e) SURFACES (confirm-first untouched everywhere; the v127 persistent suggestion box untouched): **photo wiring** (js/app.js onbWirePhoto — Preferences + Maintenance cards + onboarding) races both kinds; when the cloud disagrees with a landed local value it CORRECTS the field through the same persist() only while the field still holds exactly the local value, and says so; if the driver typed/tapped (field ≠ local value) the cloud value waits as the v127 chip — never a silent overwrite (both arms driven in the new suite, G1/G2); a gated local read keeps its plain explanation as the status, with the find announcement APPENDED when the cloud chip lands (announceFind) instead of swapped in; **both day sheets** (start prompt + end sheet) race the same way — first confident answer holds the field, the other arrival speaks in the status line, Save stays the confirm; **Fuel receipt** — gaps fill fill-only, money fields correct only while untouched-and-exactly-local (fuCloudSettled), the read time rides along; **Fuel odometer photo** and **Maintenance sticker** mirror the sheet rules per field (the sticker corrects per input: miles and date independently); **Dispatch** — both funnels (form attach + close-with-ticket) start the cloud at capture via dspBeginEarly on the smaller rendition; the escalation adopts it (the close funnel's confirm wait is now just the cloud's own ~2–5 s instead of local-then-cloud in series); a rich local read merges the settled cloud read through the same fill-only applyMerged (idempotent — every write is fill-only/absent-only). **TESTS:** NEW `tools/power-read-speed-test.js` 78/78 — engine ordering/dedupe/adoption (settle fires before the adopting onResult; identical fields; the monthly counter moves once), the manual-timer timeout bound, offline-once queueing, implausible-never-done, disabled-null; rendition pins + cap passthrough; statics for all seven call sites; and the REAL onbWirePhoto driven in a fake DOM (correction through persist, typed-value protection with the chip standing by, cloud-first chip while local still runs, agreement = one persist). Deliberate pin moves, each cited in place: power-read-integration A2 (the zero-provider-calls contract superseded by the v142 capture-time read — exactly one call, escalation count still 0) + S2 (sheet call signature); power-read-safety (safetyPowerRead call site); dispatch-intake (escalateTicketRead signature); odo-photos (slice window 2600→4600 — the handler grew the race preamble); fuel-fill (fuReadTime assignment count 2→3 — the settle path is a third fill route and carries the time too, guarded). FULL SUITE (71 suites + OCR 32/32): fail set = exactly the 3 standing baseline rows (background-day 38/1; chatbot H2 163/1; manifest-export 60/1 f1), verified against a pre-change baseline run — every other per-suite count identical. webVersion 143 → 144; index pins moved; OTA manifest regenerated. **RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly:** (i) SERVER-SIDE 502 BURSTS: ~1 in 3 probe calls 502'd in bursts on the free Gemini tier (recovers in ~a minute). The app now shows that as a fast honest failure/queue instead of minutes of spinner, but the fix for the bursts themselves lives in ~/nav/ai / the Gemini quota — a different codebase, out of this build's scope by the brief. (ii) The direct-key route's chain walk is bounded PER MODEL (20 s × up to 4 models + retry); the live route is the proxy, which is bounded per attempt as pinned. (iii) The cost trade, stated plainly: Power Read now spends ONE cloud read per photo (at capture) instead of one per local failure — that is the speed the owner ordered; the switch still turns it off entirely, and with it off every surface behaves byte-identically to v141 (beginEarly returns null). (iv) The background candidate hunt (odoHunt/nsHunt) still runs its pooled reads on the rendition after the local read — it is off the critical path now (chips arrive whenever they arrive; nothing waits on it), but it does share the phone's CPU during those seconds. (v) Machine proof only, per the standing standard: the verdict is the owner's next dash photo at his truck — the suggestion should be standing in ~2 s, before the local read would even have finished.
- **v143 — CLOSE FLOW: PHOTO DOOR + CONFIRMATION (owner's catch, Oct 17, 2026, 16:22 MDT: "I think I closed the shift wrong, I didn't upload my ticket, because there was no way to choose a picture! And there was no confirmation before it actually closed the shift!"; ships as webVersion 145 — the OTA number only moves forward).** He closed his Oct 17 shift himself and the flow he hit gave him NO ticket-photo choice and NO confirmation — one tap and it was closed, wrong. **CHARACTERIZATION (every close/complete entry point audited in code):** (1) dispatch plain Complete (`#dp_complete`) — ONE tap built the closed record, stamped it, wrote list + cleared active with raw unverified writes, logged Revenue and stopped the shift. No photo door, no confirmation. This is the flow his words describe. (2) dispatch Close with ticket photo (`#dp_close_ticket`) — already capture → confirm → verified write (`dspCloseRecord`); its one gap against the rule was inside the confirm step: a chosen photo could not be replaced there, and a hand-typed close had no way back to a photo without cancelling out. (3) the tracker End shift sheet (the "day sheet" close — `App.trackerSaveEnd` → `stopSessionAt`) — a form (end time + optional end-ODO photo) whose "Stop & save" wrote the session immediately: no ticket-photo door (the only photo on the sheet reads the odometer), no close summary, no confirmation. (4) Dispatch Cancel already confirms (native) and is not a facts close — untouched. (5) Automatic paths (dispatch-clock auto-stop, autostart/native arm) are system events, not driver closes — no confirmations bolted on, per the brief. **THE BUILD — one rule for every driver close: the close ends on a summary the driver reads, the ticket photo is offered right there, and one explicit Confirm writes:** **(a) Dispatch (modules/dispatch.js):** plain Complete now opens the SAME confirm step the ticket close ends on, in mode 'plain' (`paintPlainConfirm`): the record's own ticket photo is SHOWN ("Ticket photo already on this dispatch — it closes with it"), or its absence is stated plainly ("No ticket photo on this dispatch yet.") WITH the photo door right there — Take photo / Choose file running the shared reader, landing on the ticket confirm so the photo becomes the closing ticket exactly as if he had tapped "Close with ticket photo" first — plus "Type the ticket instead" for parity with the capture step's manual door. `paintClosePlan` serves both modes: the preview is built the way THIS confirm will close (plain = the record as it stands, ticket = the ticket being confirmed) through the ONE money assembly (`dspCompletionFor`), and the summary now carries explicit Date and Loads lines above the entry amount + hours-split note it always showed. Cancel/back writes nothing (the close state is memory-only until Confirm). Confirm (`confirmClose`'s plain arm) performs the v129 construction unchanged — `dspBuildClosed` with no new ticket, facts stamped from the same plan (`dspApplyCloseFacts`) — now through the VERIFIED `dspCloseRecord` transaction the ticket close has always used instead of the old raw writes, so a failed write closes nothing, logs nothing and says so in the view. The ticket-mode confirm gains the replace door (`#dp_cf_replace`: "↩ Use a different photo" / "📷 Add the ticket photo instead") back to the capture step — the photo stays replaceable until Confirm, nothing written. An unfinished edit draft on the dispatch now blocks plain Complete's open with the same plain words the ticket close uses (the close never silently discards an edit). **(b) Tracker End shift sheet (index.html + js/app.js):** the sheet gains a ticket-photo door on the form (label "Ticket photo — optional", Take photo + Choose file, the dispatch tickets' 640px thumb convention; the choice is previewed, replacing is tapping again, and it is held in memory only). The primary is now "Review the close →": `trackerSaveEnd` validates exactly as before (end-time parse, overnight, future clamp, ≤0 refusal, >12 h sanity confirm — all unchanged) and then moves the sheet to the REVIEW step instead of stopping: date, shift start → end with total hours, loads/trips/weight/distance as counted, the end-odometer value when one was given, "Logs to Revenue $X" priced by `endCloseSummary` — a mirror of Revenue `onSession`'s arithmetic over the same inputs (pay standard × the v137 own-rate chain, the tracker's counted seconds/quantities), the ticket photo shown or its absence stated plainly ("No ticket photo — the shift still closes. Go back to add one."), and, when a dispatch is still active, the honest line that ending the shift does not close it. Back returns to the form with every value intact and writes nothing. ONLY the review's Confirm (`App.trackerConfirmEnd`) stops: it hands the chosen photo to `stopSessionAt` through `_endTicketPhoto`, which the stop consumes (read + clear at entry, so a photo can never ride into a later close) and stamps on the session entry it saves (`entry.ticketPhoto`) beside the end-odometer stamp. If a shell ever lacks the review surface, `paintEndReview` falls back to the pre-v143 direct stop rather than stranding a validated close. The end-sheet odometer statuses now say "tap Review the close" (the button they name exists again). **MONEY: byte-identical by construction** — no computation was touched anywhere: dispatch closes run the same `dspCompletionFor` assembly and log the entry it produces (the plain close's logged entry deep-equals the plan for the closed record, suite-pinned); the tracker review's figure is display-only, and the write still happens solely in `onSession` (the new suite pins the two equal on the same session). **TESTS:** NEW `tools/close-confirm-v143-test.js` **58/58** — (A) plain Complete: tap writes nothing (list byte-identical, no revenue), the confirm shows the existing photo, the date, $304.50 and the foreman+travel split; Cancel writes nothing; Confirm completes exactly once (double-tap safe), entry deep-equals the plan; (B) plain confirm with no photo: absence stated, door present, picking a photo lands on the ticket confirm with the photo shown and the replace door standing, Confirm closes with the photo stored ONCE in tickets[] and the top-level cleared; (C) ticket confirm replace door returns to capture, nothing written; (D) tracker extractions: `stopSessionAt` stamps + consumes the handed-in photo (second close carries none), `endCloseSummary` (2 h paused, 3 tapped loads, hourly $29) == the REAL `revenue.onSession` amount for the same session, to the cent; (E) statics (sheet containers + door ids + Review primary, `trackerSaveEnd` contains no stop call, the stop lives behind `trackerConfirmEnd`, dispatch plain mode + doors, native-dialog count in dispatch.js unchanged at 3). Deliberate pin moves, each cited in place: dispatch-close (e) (plain Complete's drive gains the nothing-written assertions + the Confirm tap — its WHAT-it-logs assertions are unchanged; suite 77 → 79); dispatch-completed (f) manual Complete (same drive change; the "both close paths stamp" static holds again literally — confirmClose's two arms both call `dspApplyCloseFacts(doneRec, planRes.plan)`); live-drive D2/D5/D4b (the three plain-Complete drives confirm the close — the D5 shift-closed sentence and the manifest facts are asserted on the same outcomes as before; makeChainWorld also extracts the `_stopViaDispatch` declaration block stopSessionAt now reads `_endTicketPhoto` from — a READ of an undeclared var throws in the vm world where the old assignment-created global did not); odo-photos §9 (the sheet primary's label: "Stop & save" → "Review the close", because the button's job changed — it opens the review, the stop is the review's Confirm); chatbot-v141 F10 + navigator-v141 ship pins 144 → 145 (the standing convention). **FULL SUITE (72 suites + OCR 32/32): fail set = exactly the 3 standing baseline rows** (background-day 38/1 clock fixture; chatbot 163/1 H2 stale webVersion-104 assert; manifest-export 60/1 f1 fixture), verified against a pre-change baseline run — every other per-suite count identical except the moves above and the new suite. webVersion 144 → 145; 24 index pins moved; OTA manifest regenerated (49 files). Files changed: modules/dispatch.js, js/app.js, index.html, version.json, tools/close-confirm-v143-test.js (new), tools/dispatch-close-test.js, tools/dispatch-completed-test.js, tools/live-drive-test.js, tools/odo-photos-test.js, tools/chatbot-v141-test.js, tools/navigator-v141-test.js, BUGS.md. **RESIDUAL LIMITS, honestly:** (i) The tracker review's by-mile amount shows only when the app holds a live-km count at review time (the odometer-delta fallback `onSession` can use is priced from readings that land WITH the stop, so the review shows hours/loads/photo and no $ line rather than a guess — the logged amount is unchanged either way). (ii) A ticket photo attached at a tracker close rides the SESSION record; it does not also attach to a still-open dispatch (the review says the dispatch is still open and names its own close door) — the dispatch close remains where dispatch tickets live, by the one-photo-one-home convention. (iii) Machine proof only, per the standing standard: the verdict is the owner's next real close — plain Complete or the End shift sheet should meet him with the summary and the photo door, and nothing should close before his Confirm.
